# Open Threads

## Immediate volume promises

- With no charter and no contractor, can the Lantern Reach node be brought back into somebody's maintenance before the flood, and can Kellan's falsified gauge be exposed without destroying the field the crew came to repair?
- Why does Aldren's fieldbook carry a procedure in living wood, and why does the bead carry a date later than the disappearance?
- Who wrote the works order, who reads it, and why is a branch on no local map still somebody's job?
- Can Tova's Dryfoot Trust and Lantern Reach keep a line alive when the holding holding it has no charter and no return?
- What happens on the twenty-seventh, and does the cooperative cut its own branch because a table says it is due?

## State after Batch 0001 (Chapters 1–10, days 1–11)

These are live, dated, and owed. Seven days to the eighteenth.

### Clocks the next batch must pay

### Open questions carried forward

- **Who scheduled the cut at Weir Row, and why does no local map show the branch?** The answer is a half-sheet of a table older than the cooperative's charter, and Bray does not have the rest of it. **Sera has now said it out loud in Ch 8** — she has seen the shape of the table, she has refused twice by name (where she saw it, what it is), and she asked for both refusals to be recorded. This thread is live in a way it was not before.
- **Why does the fieldbook's bead carry a work sequence and a date later than the disappearance?** Marek has written it down as a copying error and asked that it be treated as one. That wording is the only protection he has. Ch 46 is where Sera says what the pattern is: a target pattern, a work order written in advance. Nobody names who wrote it.
- **What was Aldren doing at Weir Row eight years ago, and who put his name in an old schedule log?** Not answerable from Lantern Reach. Batch 0001 plants only the impossible date.
- **What is Kellan's side draw actually supplying, and whose drinking water is it?** The charter permits up to 400 turns/day, so the taking is legal; what is not yet in the record is the paper on the shape of free offers that Tova would not describe in the Chapter 4 seed house. **It carries no number, no date and no place in the prose.** Do not attach one to it in Movement 2 or a reader will date the collapse.
- **What is living at Weir Row, and does it want the dead flow back or only the living things out of it?** The pathhound lost the route it could feel and went to look at the water in the drainage channel. Nobody has been back to find out what it found. Lissa's rule stands for the rest of the volume: it is herding, not hunting, and the only thing that moves it is a change in where the water goes.
- **Can a cooperative with no contractor and £5 11 build a real seal in seven days, and who pays for a shared draw's return?**
- **Whose handprint is on the weir stones?** Marek's, and he does not remember putting it there. Do not resolve this casually.

## State after Batch 0002 (Chapters 11–20, days 12–20)

The cooperative has **no charter**. The pump still runs. A hand-built holding seal is standing over a wound with a weep at its low corner, and nobody is watching the head except a rotating list of four people.

| The weep at the six o'clock corner. A quarter of an inch, measured, not a leak; half an inch is where Sera stops calling it a weep. A leak under sacking goes into the housing, not the pit | Ch 19 | Do not resolve before Ch 41; the flood is decided by it |

### Open questions carried forward

- **Who scheduled the cut at Weir Row, and why does no local map show the branch?** Now a **printed district works schedule of ninety rows** that the cooperative was given as a courtesy and has handed back unread. Sera has admitted to a district clerk that she has worked with schedule forms and has refused three times by name. The two marked-done dates in row forty-one are the two stamped cut faces, and the **order date is older than Marek's oldest cut**, so the schedule is on time and not slipping. Nobody has seen a table bigger than a district's.
- **Why does the fieldbook's bead carry a work sequence, and why is the date later than the disappearance?** On the twentieth Sera read the same node and got **cut, wait, cut** — the order the Weir Row wood is in — and called it a procedure stored in living wood and not a message. That is one step short of the Ch 46 answer and must stay one step short. Marek's protection, *probably a copying error, to be treated as one*, is untouched.
- **What is Aldren doing at Weir Row eight years ago, and who put his name in an old schedule log?** Not answerable from Lantern Reach. The twenty-seventh may put a Crown crew in front of it.
- **What is Kellan's side draw actually supplying, and whose drinking water is it?** It is now the *only licensed draw on the node*, since the cooperative's lapsed on the eighteenth. The Ch 4 paper on the shape of free offers still carries no number, no date and no place and must not acquire one.
- **What is living on the Fennmere dead flow?** The second, adult pathhound holds it in Ch 23–25. Nothing in Batch 0002 restages the weir night and no animal appears in the batch.
- **Whose handprint is on the weir stones?** Unresolved and not to be resolved casually. Sera checks Marek's arm on the eighth, the twelfth and the twentieth and it is still nothing.
- **Whose return keeps the seal open?** Nobody's, yet. The certificate's monthly clause is a return made of examination. The compost is £80 a month and there is none, and the graft in Ch 21–25 is going to want one.

### Volume 01 plan locks

Set in `outline/volume-01.md` and the drafted chapters. See `state/continuity.md` for the numbers, records, and stage accounting.

- **The eighteenth day was one deadline, not two, and the cooperative lost it.** The charter expiry and the annual inspection were the same date, the renewal was not granted, and the charter lapsed at midnight. The deciding hearing comes later, in Chapters 46–48.
- **The loss is on a technicality, and it is the cooperative's own doing.** The seal is real, correctly logged by four named hands under a licensed engineer, and is not contested by the district as workmanship. The record of its completion was written at nine the next morning instead of four the previous afternoon, by the person responsible, alone, because he wanted to know whether it held first. Anneke Roos wrote the rule that forbids it on the fourteenth. It is in the batch's own log, in the cooperative's own hand.
- **Volume 01 delivers the target-pattern answer and no more:** what is in the wood is a work order, not a memory of a season that has not happened. Nobody in this volume names the Engine, the office, False Season, or Iona Vey.
- **Two different pathhounds.** The weir animal in Chapter 9 was redirected by changing the dead flow, not commanded, and it left on its own. The Fennmere-area animal in Chapters 23–25 settles on the dead flow and holds it.
- **The lost compost return is the mechanism of the midpoint reversal.** The flock lost in Chapter 9 is why the Chapter 21–25 graft can succeed and fail at the same time.
- **The bypass is a note in the margin (Ch 10) and a second note beside it (Ch 16), and is still unbuilt when the flood arrives.** It must not be quietly funded or built early.
- **The public pressure log and monthly report were voted by the cooperative in Chapter 10, maintained by it in Chapters 12–20, and adopted by the Crown as charter conditions in Chapter 47.**
- **Nia leaves in Chapter 20 and does not return within the volume.**
- **Marek is Stage 0 at the end of Batch 0002, with two contacts behind him, no third, and no rootmark.** Stage 1 arrives at the end of Volume 01, unreliable and dependent on a licensed witness. The first skin rootmark belongs to Volume 02.

## State after Batch 0003 (Chapters 21–30, days 21–30)

The repair worked. Nineteen acres of the outer field are standing. The cooperative has signed a document saying it cannot prove what it knows, it is being billed £60 10 for a low quarter it cannot reach the branch of, and three acres of its engineer's own ground are gone for two seasons. **No charter. No contractor. No license of its own. No money: £4 5 in the box and an unfed bed.**

### Open questions carried forward

- **Who scheduled the third cut, and why is there a hold on row forty-one that was released on Monday?** The works extract is in a clerk's satchel in Fennmere, has been handed back, and the district has now stated on the counter that it will never be produced. **A hold that is marked and dated in the same column and the same hand as a date, released on a Monday, by a job sheet nobody may see, is a new question and it belongs to Volume 02 or later.** Nobody in Volume 01 may answer it.
- **What is in the wood that made the fieldbook say *below is not yours*?** Sera will not say whether it is a description or an instruction, has forbidden the phrase *the book told us to* in any document, and has stated a rule of her own that she refuses to number or explain. **The Aldren thread is untouched by this batch** and *probably a copying error, to be treated as one* still stands as his only protection.
- **Why does the fieldbook's bead carry a work sequence, and why is the date later than the disappearance?** Untouched. Ch 20's *cut, wait, cut* is still one step short of the Ch 46 answer.
- **What was Aldren doing at Weir Row eight years ago, and who put his name in an old schedule log?** Not answerable from Lantern Reach. The twenty-seventh put a Crown crew two hundred yards from the branch he cut by hand and did not ask a question.
- **Whose return keeps the seal open?** Still nobody's. The monthly examination is a return made of examination and the bed it is supposed to feed has an empty bin. **The certificate's return clause falls due on the first of the coming month with no compost behind it**, and that is a document failing in public with a twenty-three-year-old's name on it.
- **What is Kellan's side draw actually supplying, and whose drinking water is it?** It is the only licensed draw on the node, his branch has never been above 300, and Renn Tallow says in one flat line that the branch below has had nothing coming up it since the spring before last and every man in the low quarter knows it. **Nobody has yet put those two facts in one document**, and the document that would is a hearing officer's.
- **Whose handprint is on the weir stones?** Unresolved. Sera has checked Marek's arm four times and it is still nothing.
- **Is Corvin Slee's report a fact or an accident?** He dated it himself and the wells went off on the twenty-first, which is the day the cooperative let water into a new run. **The cooperative can time a pail and cannot produce a table, and the date is going to be the district's whole case.** Mara's own book says *the new run is fed from below* and a licensed engineer wrote *below is not yours*, and both are in the district's hands.

### Volume 01 plan locks

Set in `outline/volume-01.md` and the drafted chapters. See `state/continuity.md` for the numbers, records, and stage accounting.

- **The midpoint reversal has landed and it is irreversible.** A licensed graft works, nineteen acres stand up, and the return that made it work comes out of a branch two hundred yards away and nine miles upstream. **Nobody in the volume may now describe the repair as a success without naming the bill.**
- **The bill exists and it is about damage the cooperative did not do alone and cannot prove.** £60 10, fourteen days, no appeal. **The Crown's own schedule is structurally unavailable, not concealed, and that is the political discovery of the movement.**
- **Tova's refusal is correct and Marek let it stand and lost three acres.** Neither is repaired later in the volume. **The outer strip is lost in this volume, before the flood.**
- **The bypass is unbuilt and stays unbuilt through Ch 41.**
- **The public pressure log and monthly report are still the cooperative's own obligation and are not yet charter conditions.** The Crown adopts them in Ch 47. **Do not let a district clerk or Bray treat the cooperative's books as an accepted correction in the meantime** — the claim in the log was moved and lost 9–22 in Ch 4, and Dunmar filed a cause on the seventeenth and never a figure.
- **Nia is ten days gone. Her absence has to work against Marek through Movements 4 and 5.**
- **Marek is Stage 0 at the end of Batch 0003, with two contacts behind him, no third, and no rootmark.** He offered his body as a return in Ch 29 and was refused. Stage 1 arrives at the end of Volume 01. The first skin rootmark belongs to Volume 02.
- **The two pathhounds are two animals.** The weir animal left on the tenth and did not come back. The Fennmere collie moved nine miles on the twenty-seventh because a channel came alive, and it is holding a line, and Joss Pyle brings it milk.

## State after Batch 0004 (Chapters 31–40, days 31–40)

Movement 4 is drafted and its promise is paid: **the cooperative kept the record, the record is what is used against them, the woman who kept it stopped it from being taken, and she lost the clean record for it.** The liability has landed slowly and on paper. **The instrument is safe, the sitting is dated, the seed annex is signed, the storm is forecast, and the bypass is unbuilt.**

### Open questions carried forward

- **What is under the toe of the windbreak, and what happens to the settlement if the burn goes ahead without its answer.** The cooperative now knows the long line runs there and may not say so, and the Assemtry will hold it against the day. **Nobody in Volume 01 may name the Assemtry in a book.**
- **What is in the wood that made the fieldbook say *below is not yours*.** Untouched. Sera's unnumbered fourth refusal stands and nobody asked her anything this batch.
- **Why does the bead carry a work sequence, and why is the date later than the disappearance?** Untouched, and answered one step further in Ch 46. *(This line is the state as it stood after Batch 0004. **The volume's last Fieldbook panel was spent in Ch 46 and the allowance is now zero** — see *State after Batch 0005*.)*
- **Who scheduled the third cut, and why was a hold on row forty-one released on a Monday?** Untouched. The schedule is still unproduced, and the district has now certified the *fact* of three executions without producing a line of it. **Belongs to Volume 02 or later; nobody in Volume 01 may answer it.**
- **Whose handprint is on the weir stones?** Unresolved. Sera has not looked at his arm a fifth time and does not appear in this batch.
- **What is living on the Fennmere dead flow?** The second pathhound was reported at a gate on the sixth of this month and nothing was written underneath the report. **If the bypass is ever built it will move, and nobody in this volume may say what moves it, and nobody may use the animal as a symbol.** The weir animal does not return.
- **Ch 29 narrates days 29–34 while Ch 30 is day 30.** The overlap is **still open**. Batch 0004 avoided it by using the crust as it stood on the Sunday, the twenty-ninth, and nothing in Chapters 31–40 depends on closing it. **Recorded, not fixed.**

### Volume 01 plan locks, restated for the flood

- **The midpoint reversal stands and the liability has landed on paper.** A licensed graft works, nineteen acres stand, and the cooperative's own honest record is the district's best exhibit. **Nobody may describe the repair as a success without naming the bill.**
- **The bill is sixty pounds ten, and the cooperative does not dispute the amount.** It disputes the cause. **If the cause is lost the money is owed in full** — that trade was made in a stranger's kitchen on the fourth of this month, in Marek's own hand.
- **Lissa Vale wins the instrument and the cooperative loses the clean record, and neither is a victory.** She is not sorry and has said so. Do not make her sorry.
- **The public pressure log and monthly report are still the cooperative's own obligation and are not yet charter conditions.** The Crown adopts them in Ch 47. The certified copy in a Fennmere file is **not** an accepted correction and the district has said so in a register.
- **Nia is twenty days gone.** Her absence has to work against Marek through the flood.
- **The seed bed is at three and a half degrees, the bin is empty, the March's tin is in the press, and £79 16 a month of compost does not exist.** The seed annex is signed and the crop may not come up.
- **Marek's body is not on the table.** He is Stage 0. He offered it once, on the twenty-seventh of last month, and was refused in eleven seconds, and he has not offered it again in twelve days.

## Historical and political threads

- The Quiet Break's engineering origin and the Crown's concealment must be revealed in stages.
- False Season must be shown as a maintained control pattern rather than natural weather.
- Sera's Brinewake confession, the falsified relay log, and the missing compensation records need evidence, restitution, and a public cost. She has not been asked and has not offered.
- The First Continuity covenant's prohibition on permanent binding must be recovered and compared with later charter law.
- Iona's three succession offers must remain distinct: technical co-caretaker, conditional succession, and final permanent link.

## Relationship and consequence threads

- Marek and Tova must learn to disagree in public without making each other foolish; consent must be a recorded part of anchor work and maintenance. Nothing tender has happened and nothing should be allowed to lean that way in Volume 01.
- Lissa's refusal to forgive Marek for taking the capital contract needs a later, non-absolution beat. Her custody of the fieldbook is not forgiveness and is not nothing.
- Halden's professional veto needs a specific reconciliation after the Charter of Many Wells.
- Tarin's critique of central control must remain separate from his responsibility for deaths; his post-climax custody is planned.
- Nia's route out of Tarrow Fen must become freely chosen work rather than a debt repaid to the family. The list she asks for in Chapter 10 is the first move.

## Final-volume dependencies

- Regional replacement seedhearts must be planted and load-tested before the caretaker handoff can begin.
- The living control seed recovered in Volume 06 can open local valves but cannot command the running Engine without a caretaker link; its limitation must stay visible as the coalition builds the replacement.
- The final network must use named capacities—local conduits, seed-route bridges, and regional bridges—without making one bridge the central authority.
- Some regions will remain damaged or refuse reconnection; do not erase that cost to make the ending look complete.

A full continuity, card-beat and pacing review was applied in place. **The review found three things wrong in the state files that the prose was right about, and three wrong in the prose that the state files had already recorded as delivered** — the serials, Sera's Ch 8 beat, and the contact length. All fourteen numbered fixes are listed in `state/continuity.md` under *Fixes applied to the drafted Batch 0001 on review*.
- Chapters 11–20 are drafted in `workspace/volume-01/batch-0002/` and summarized in `state/chapter-summaries.md`, with the batch's new numbers and locks in `state/continuity.md` under *Numbers and records established in Chapters 11–20* and *New locks from the drafted Batch 0002*.
- Chapters 21–30 are drafted in `workspace/volume-01/batch-0003/` and summarized in `state/chapter-summaries.md`, with the batch's new numbers in *Numbers and records established in Chapters 21–30*, its locks in *New locks from the drafted Batch 0003*, its day clock and month-boundary dating table in *Batch 0003 day clock*, and its five proposed canon changes in *Proposed canon changes recorded, not silently applied — Batch 0003*. **A review pass was run and applied in place; the fourteen fixes, the month-boundary rule, and one structural date defect found and deliberately left open are all in `state/continuity.md` under *Fixes applied to the drafted Batch 0003 on review* and the section that follows it. No scene was replaced and no planned plot was changed.**
- **Open and unresolved after the repair: Ch 29 narrates days 29–34 while Ch 30 is day 30**, so the two chapters overlap and Ch 30 describes a three-acre crust that Ch 29 dates to the Wednesday. This is a chapter-structure problem, not a slip, and it is recorded rather than fixed. **A later writer must resolve it in prose before the Batch 0004 chapters are treated as final.** Cheapest fix: keep Ch 29's Sunday-to-Friday span and move Ch 30's three-acres paragraph back to the crust already visible on the Sunday. Ch 30's date, Sera's fourth arm check, the eleven-hundred-word statement, and the form's fourteen days may not move.
- **Dates in Chapters 1–40 are now arithmetically true, and the rule is in `state/continuity.md` under *Batch 0003 day clock*. Months run thirty days: season days 1–30 are October and day 31 is the first of the next month.** A day in 1–20 is dated by its plain number, *of last month*, or *of this season* — never *of this month*. **A day in 21–30 is the plain number only — the twenty-first … the thirtieth — never the first, second, third … of a month.** The Batch 0003 pass introduced a boundary that made the month twenty days long, and it was carried into ten chapters of Movement 3; the Batch 0004 review found it and the fix pass has corrected every instance, including five quoted records and one chapter title. A later writer should not reintroduce either version of the error. **October is load-bearing:** it is named four times in speech in Chs 24, 28 and 29, it is the name of the previous month, and it is what makes the flock's departure *the tenth of October*. Do not contradict it, do not name the month days 21–30 fall in, and **never attach a month to an entry, a notice, a license sheet or a letter.** The state files' old claim that no month is named in Chapters 1–30 was false and has been corrected.
- Chapters 31–40 are drafted in `workspace/volume-01/batch-0004/` and summarized in `state/chapter-summaries.md`, with the batch's new numbers in *Numbers and records established in Chapters 31–40*, its locks in *New locks from the drafted Batch 0004*, its day clock in *Batch 0004 day clock*, and its seven proposed canon changes in *Proposed canon changes recorded, not silently applied — Batch 0004*. **A review pass was run and applied in place; its three blocking and six non-blocking findings and the length note are in `reviews/batch-0004.findings.md` and in `state/continuity.md` under *The Batch 0004 review pass — independent, and what it changed*. No scene was replaced and no planned plot was changed.** **The twenty-item list at the head of the fixes section is the writer's own self-check, not a review, and is now labelled as such.** The Batch 0003 structural date defect (Ch 29 narrating days 29–34 against Ch 30 on the thirtieth) **remains open**; Batch 0004 avoided it rather than closing it, using the crust as it stood on the Sunday, the twenty-ninth.
- **The prompt for Batch 0005 now exists: `workspace/volume-01/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`.** It is **Chapters 41–49, nine chapters**, and it is the last batch of the volume: `outline/volume-01.md` puts the flood in Chs 41–43, the volume climax in 44–46, the hearing in 46–48 and the resolution in 49. It carries the corrected day clock (day 41 onward), the sitting on the twenty-first of the month as season day 51, the rain from the twelfth, the reed gauge's history, the unspent Fieldbook panel, and the length instruction from the review: **nine chapters, and the house average must come down.**
- **Length — known and accepted, and much improved.** Batch 0001 ran 2,700–5,300 words per chapter against the outline's 2,200–3,200. **Batch 0002 runs 2,500–4,200, ten chapters and about 34,100 words total**, against a 28,000 target: a residual overrun of about 22%, concentrated in Ch 15 (five scenes in one working day), Ch 19 (four complete scenes in the batch's technical climax), Ch 16 and Ch 17. The hard warning in the phase prompt was not to open five chapters at 4,500 and the longest is 4,200. A review pass trimmed roughly 900 words out of the four longest chapters. **A later writer should trim rather than pad, and should not lengthen Ch 15, 16, 17 or 19.** **Batch 0003 runs 2,669–3,741, ten chapters and 31,099 in total, about 11% over the 28,000 target, and four chapters breach the 3,200 outline ceiling — Ch 27 (3,226), Ch 28 (3,335), Ch 29 (3,247) and Ch 30 (3,741) — so it is one chapter over the prompt's once-and-only-once allowance for a long chapter. Trim, do not pad; do not lengthen Chs 26, 27, 28, 29 or 30.**

So the district has still never been given a corrected draw figure, only a cause. Movement 3 must not quietly convert the open book into an accepted correction.

## State after Batch 0005 (Chapters 41–49, days 41–52) — VOLUME 01 IS CLOSED

**The flood came, the corner was opened by hand, the outer strip went under on purpose, the four records were put on a bench in the mud, the wood was named a work order, and the hearing produced a conditional charter that expires at the end of the season.** The volume's surface answer — a Crown schedule, not the weather — is paid. The volume's deeper answer — a **target pattern, a work order written into living wood in advance** — is paid. **The volume's cost is paid and none of it is refunded.** Nothing below is closed.

### The new question for Volume 02, and how to keep it answerable

**Why does the fen's scarroot carry a work order in Aldren Vale's hand, dated after the disappearance, for a branch on no current map — and who put his name in the old schedule log as the person who authorised the cut?**

This question is now answerable from the state files without re-reading the manuscript, and a Volume 02 writer should not have to reconstruct it. What Volume 01 put on the record for it:

1. **The date.** On the fieldbook's last page, in Aldren's hand, a line of figures and a column head: *confirmed — set — 4*, and a year eight months after the disappearance. **Marek wrote *probably a copying error; to be treated as one* into his own finding and asked for that wording himself, and it is still the only protection he has.** It has not been withdrawn and nobody has tested it.
2. **The branch.** The Weir Row branch is on no current map of this holding, carries the Crown sign *BRANCH MAINTENANCE — DISTRICT SCHEDULE. NO LOCAL HOLDER.*, and has been cut three times: on the night of the tenth of last month by a district crew, on the twenty-fourth of last month by Marek with his own shears at the copper seam, and on the twenty-seventh of last month to specification, square, at the bottom of the opened ground, where it should have landed. **Three stamped faces, all N-2214/K.**
3. **The work order in the wood.** On the seventeenth of the month the instrument gave its own three plain lines at the copper seam, read by a licensed reader and written into a book and a district file: *Cut the upper and leave the lower. Wait a season, and the ground will come back. Cut again, and put your name to it.* **The same reader read it and a cut face at Weir Row and said plainly that what is in the bead is a work order — a shape set down in advance, in the order a man works in when he means to bring a thing about. A target pattern. The word for a target pattern is not a prophecy; it is a specification.** Sera Quill has seen the structure of one before, has not wanted to say so for twenty-three years, and **will not say where she saw it, what wrote it, or why a pattern of that shape would be put into living wood at all.** Those three are in a district file as questions. **Nobody in Volume 01 named who, what, or why, and that is a rule, not an omission.**
4. **The name in the box.** On the seventeenth, on a bench in the mud, in front of a district inspector, Kellan Rusk gave the name that goes in the authorising box on a district works order, and it is **an office and not a man: schedule clerk, fen drains.** The post has existed longer than Kellan; the man in it has changed four times that he knows of; **he does not come out. He signs the paper. The paper goes to a store. The store gives out the kit.** Kellan's reason for saying it: *if a thing is a shape and not a man, then the shape is public, and if the shape is not public then the man never has to answer for it.* **Sera Quill heard it and said nothing**, and Lissa Vale entered that she said nothing and did not enter why, **because she has answered that question in writing three times this season and has got shorter every time and a seed house is not going to be the voice that asks her a fourth time. Nobody asked her again in that yard, then or afterwards.** The name is now in four books in four places: the seed house book, the cooperative's book, the certified standing pressure log in Perrin Osse's copy, and a copy going to Cray.
5. **The half of the question Volume 01 did not touch.** Nobody in Volume 01 has seen a schedule log, a name in a schedule log, or a Crown record that carries a person's name against an authorisation. The district **will not produce the works schedule and that is structural, not concealment** — the table carries a column saying whether the work is Crown-held, and producing it is an admission the district does not make to a body holding no charter. **The answer to the second half arrives in Volume 02 with the first Crown paperwork the cooperative is now entitled to demand, and it does not arrive from Lantern Reach.**
6. **The route in.** The cooperative holds a **conditional charter**, made on the twenty-first, **expiring at the end of the season**, and under it a district may require records. **That is the door.** Volume 02's first Crown paperwork should arrive as a consequence of the charter, not as a favor, and the file is open and the Directorate has reserved the right to reopen it on any new report — so the paperwork can also arrive as an instrument of pressure. **The pressure and the door are the same door and Volume 02 should not pretend otherwise.**

### Clocks Volume 02 opens with, all of them live

| Clock | Where it stands at the end of Volume 01 | What it owes |
|---|---|---|
| **The conditional charter** | made on the twenty-first, four clauses and a fifth sheet, **for the season, expiring at the end of the season without notice** | The first thing it requires is the **monthly examination on the first of next month, ten days after it was made**, and it may be under a foot of fen. **Gael Senn has said she will be there and has written nothing in advance, in front of the officer, and the officer minuted it** |
| **The leak** | half an inch and a sixteenth at the six o'clock corner, under the fen's own head and not the load stated, water passing into the housing, pit not rising, **a leak and not a weep and not mended** | Nobody can mend it under sacking at load, and the only way to take the load off is to stop the pump, which stops the bed. **This is the volume's standing unsolved engineering problem and it is not a mystery** |
| **The return main** | clause three of the charter: to be made to **forty-one rods of twelve inch** by a date to be fixed, and fed at not less than the specified hundredweight a month. **The three hundred and ninety-nine hundredweight shortfall is carried forward and not discharged** | The third exchange requirement is still the hole in the only licensed work in the volume, **it is now a charter condition rather than a note**, and no later chapter may quietly fill it |
| **The compost** | bought at four shillings, **about a twentieth of what the graft was designed to be fed**, four pounds five in a fortnight twice out of sixteen households | Marek's own margin note: nobody will see the starve from the top of the field; it will be visible in the second week of next August; by then it will be somebody else's log |
| **The bed** | **three and a half degrees**, measured twice with a soil iron on the first, the fourteenth and the twentieth. **A hundredweight is worth about half a degree; twenty-one hundredweight and a quarter is worth about a tenth; a soil iron cannot read a tenth** | Tova Reed's rule: the hundredweight goes in the ledger and the degree waits, and she will not write a degree she cannot stand behind |
| **The March's tin** | still in the press under the ledger, **thirty-three days at risk on the twenty-second**, unmoved, with the seed house's undertaking that it will not be moved and the trust's that it will not be asked | The dry-land line is **still lost**. The first row of the March's own line is thinner by **fourteen pounds ten**, entered on the annex against the trust's interest |
| **The schedule of damage** | **sixty pounds ten, entered and not owed, not discharged, not remitted, not reduced.** The file stays open. **The emergency clause stays live** | A debt is enforceable against a body and a body is a thing with a charter. **The only remedy the district has is the emergency clause, on a report, at a time of its choosing** |
| **The Directorate's reservation** | **the right to reopen the file on any new report**, minuted, and not tradeable | **This is the handoff to Volume 02, where the Directorate stops being pressure and becomes structure** |
| **Nia's letter** | **not delivered, and it is back.** It came into the seed house **sealed, by hand, on the nineteenth of this month**, unopened, brought by a person who went on up the road and did not stop. Nobody in the fen opened it or wrote down why it was not delivered, because the person who carried it did not give a reason and nobody went after her. It is in the open drawer of the seed house table with the levy papers and the rent book. **Nia herself is not in the volume and does not come back** | **Whether she ever hands it over belongs to nobody but her and is still unsettled, and her absence has to work against Marek in Volume 02 without anybody explaining it to him** |
| **The three acres** | under water for a week from the sixteenth of the month, sour, will not crop until the third season after this one, **and entered in the cooperative's book as a decision and not as a charge** | Not repaid in this volume and **not repayable by anybody** |
| **The nineteen acres** | standing, and the new run green, and four willow sets alive, **and the graft about to be starved for want of compost** | The first harvest comes in short and real |

### Carried forward, not closed — the eight live threads

1. **The pressure pulse.** One beat in about four seconds, out of the sump, **logged eight times**, always *cause not established*, always with no name, the eighth on the eleventh of the month with **nine in the quarter hour, four of them together, no licensed person present, and nothing written underneath it by Gael Senn's decision and with her name on the decision.** **Nobody in Volume 01 named or explained it and nobody in Volume 02 may do so cheaply.** It is undiagnosed and it is the slowest thing in the book.
2. **The third exchange requirement.** Still missing on the one licensed work in the volume; **now a charter condition**; **the shortfall is 399 hundredweight a month, seventy-nine pounds sixteen against a specification of eighty pounds and a box of four pounds five.** A later chapter may not quietly fill it.
3. **The never-produced works schedule.** The district will not produce it, and that is structural: the table carries a Crown-held column and producing it is an admission the district does not make to a body holding no charter. The district **has** certified a fact about its own completion record — **three stamped faces against N-2214/K** — **without producing a page of the table.** **No villain may reverse this.** What Volume 02 now has that Volume 01 did not is a charter, and a charter is a document that can be used to demand a record.
4. **The long line and the settlement with no seat.** About two hundred yards of wild root line under the south embankment windbreak, running to the weir and on into ground that is theirs, known to about ninety people with **no charter, no seat and no name on any roll.** The cooperative holds **only the fact of a refusal and not its terms**, and the terms are not held by that body. **The burn lapsed on the twelfth by its own terms and the long line is intact, and the settlement is not named in any book and nobody in Tarrow Fen has standing to ask that it be named.** The one word *seat* was refused. **Do not name the settlement.**
5. **The pathhound.** The report of the sixth of the month is in three places — the open book, Perrin Osse's certified copy, and the copy going to the March road. **The collie came up the fen road on the nineteenth, stood in the gateway, would not come in, and went back up the road at about the eleventh hour, and Gael Senn entered her decision to write nothing about it with her name on the decision and not the animal's.** The low quarter has walked and chalked the line of the standing iron on its own hand and at its own cost, on the nineteenth and the twentieth, and **Marek did not go and said in the book that nobody in this yard is going to walk it.** **Nobody may interpret the animal, nobody may use it as a symbol, and the weir animal does not return.**
6. **Kellan's letter, and Kellan's monitored charter.** The letter to K. Rusk, Fennmere pump house, to be delivered by hand, **sealed on the twentieth of last month, not delivered, and returned to the seed house unopened on the nineteenth of this month by the person who carried it.** His private charter survives with six conditions and he is a witness and not redeemed; **he is sixty-one and does not appear again in Volume 01.** **He has also broken one of his own four conditions and reported it himself** — he was told what the first reader read before he read the node — and the sealed envelope that condition protected **is never to be opened by anybody, at any time, and is not.** Volume 02 may not open it and may not treat it as available evidence.
7. **Nia.** **Not in Volume 01 after the twentieth day of last month, and not brought back in its last nine chapters.** The letter came back sealed and the reason was not given and is not written down. **Her absence has to work against Marek through Volume 02**, and the letter is hers to deliver or not to deliver. **Nothing in the state files may say she came back, and nothing may say she decided not to deliver it** — neither is established, and the second is hers and not the state's to say.
8. **Sera Quill's fourth refusal.** A rule she made for herself nineteen years ago in a different office, that **a book does not get to be a reason.** She does not name what it is about, does not give it a number, and has not opened it in any of the forty-nine chapters. On the seventeenth of the month she heard the name of a district office and said nothing. **Do not number it, do not explain it, and do not let anybody ask her about it before Volume 03.**

### Two things that are now facts and are not mysteries

- **A district works order is signed by an office and not by a man**, and the man in the post does not come out, and the paper goes to a store, and the store gives out the kit. **This is Volume 01's last political discovery and it is a shape and not a villain**, and Volume 02 must not turn it into a man in a room without earning it.
- **A schedule of damage against a body with no charter is a sum that is entered and not owed**, and the district's only remedy is the emergency clause on a report at a time of its choosing. **The cooperative now has a charter, which means the arithmetic has changed and nobody has noticed yet.**

### The two Volume 01 defects, now both closed at the close pass

**The Ch 29 / Ch 30 overlap is closed in prose.** Ch 29's crust now comes up on the Sunday in one sheet and stands on the edge of the hole, and its banks begin to come away at the top on the Monday and roll on the Friday, which is what Chapters 30, 31, 40, 43 and 47 already said. **Ch 30's closing field walk was rewritten in its own words, which also removed six paragraphs cloned from Ch 31.** Ch 30's date, the fourth arm check, the eleven-hundred-word statement and the form's fourteen days did not move.

**The quotation-mark defect is closed.** 221 closing marks were added across 28 chapters — 219 at paragraph starts plus two mid-paragraph instances in Chapters 20 and 37 — and a second audit then removed **two surplus pairs** in Chapters 26 and 35, which are the one fault a per-chapter count cannot see, because a closing mark with nothing open pairs up as an empty quotation. **Every chapter now balances at 5,106 marks and 2,553 pairs, with no closing mark that has no opening and no paragraph that ends while a speech is open. There is nothing mechanical left to do in Volume 01.** A later writer must not reintroduce either pattern, must not read a speech that runs over paragraphs as missing its closing mark, **and must use straight quotation marks and straight apostrophes throughout** — decided at the close and recorded in `bible/terminology.md`, with the multi-paragraph speech rule written beside it.

### Phase handoff, Volume 01 → Volume 02

- Chapters 1–49 are drafted in `workspace/volume-01/batch-0001/` through `batch-0005/`, and summarized one compact entry per chapter in `state/chapter-summaries.md`. Volume 01 is **complete and closed**.
- **Batch 0005 has now been reviewed by hand and repaired in place.** The findings are in `reviews/batch-0005.findings.md`, the record of what changed is in `state/continuity.md` under *The Batch 0005 review pass — by hand, and what it changed*, and the pipeline attempt that produced them is in `logs/batch-0005.review.log`. The review phase's standing failure to load the `novel-reviewer` subagent is unresolved and is a controller-owned problem that was not fixed here.
- Batch 0005's day clock, the day-to-chapter map actually used, its new numbers, its locks, the closed-volume facts, the spelling decision and its canon changes are all in `state/continuity.md` under *Batch 0005*.
- **Volume 01 is closed and has been closed properly, and it has then been audited a second time.** The close-up is `reviews/volume-01.findings.md` and the record of every change is in `state/continuity.md` under *The Volume 01 close pass*, whose §8 is the second audit. The volume-wide mechanical work is **done and verified twice**: the quotation-mark balance is closed at zero, the day clock is clean in all forty-nine chapters at **twenty-four violations repaired, twelve found by the first pass and twelve by the second**, every load-bearing figure recomputes, the duplication scan is clean apart from deliberate house style, **the spelling is decided once as US and the quotation marks once as straight, both applied and both written into `bible/terminology.md`**, and the Ch 29 / Ch 30 overlap is closed in prose. **The Fieldbook panels are accounted for: four in the volume, at Chapters 6, 19, 24 and 46, one in each of Batches 0001, 0002, 0003 and 0005 and none in Batch 0004, and the allowance is zero.** **The close-up now also carries the relationship and power progressions, item by item, at §11 and §12, which the first pass of the close omitted and the close prompt required.**
- **What Volume 02 inherits, unchanged by the close pass:** the eight live threads above, the two facts that are not mysteries, the standing prohibitions, and the six figures and instruments in `state/continuity.md` under *Numbers and records established in Chapters 41–49*.
- The Volume 01 close phase is **complete and retired**; `workspace/volume-02/volume-close/PROMPT.md` carries its `.done` marker and was not edited afterwards.

## State after Volume 02 Batch 0001 (Chapters 50–59, days 53–64) — MOVEMENT 1

**The charter's second clause started working on the cooperative and the volume opened on the fact that the cooperative's own open book is now a filing, at its own request, and cannot be withdrawn.** Movement 1 delivered the door Volume 01 promised — the works extract, the *Crown-held* column, one certified page of a district register of authorisations, and a legend on a Crown map — and it paid the handoff's promise in the volume's own currency: **a document proving the work was authorised, and no document at all saying by whom it was authorised, because the thing that says by whom it was authorised is the fieldbook and the fieldbook is on a table in a seed house and may not be opened in an officer's room.** Nothing below is closed.

### The new question for Volume 02, and how to keep it answerable

**Who altered the maps and the seed records enough to make a bridge between this fen and the dry Salt March legal — and who was paid for the alteration?**

Volume 02 Batch 0001 has earned the **shape** of it and none of its content, and a later writer should not have to reconstruct it from the manuscript. What is now on the record:

1. **A branch on no current map is maintained in a district schedule, and the map says so in a legend.** **The legend's ninth mark is *unmapped root line, maintained in schedule*, it is used four times on the sheet, and one of the four is the Weir Row branch.** The mark is **not a warning to the holder. It is a handover to whoever is not the holder.** A district may print that fact on a map in five colors and sell it to a hundred and forty seed buyers every winter, and every act of doing so is correct, timely and structural.
2. **The *Crown-held* column.** **The extract has six columns and not one head printed over any of them. The word is the sixth word of the caption printed in the line of type at the top of the sheet, in the same type as the entries, in the same line as the form number and the place for a date — which is how a hundred people and one licensed reader read past it for a season.** The sixth column has been called a third execution by everybody in this yard since the spring and holds a letter in every row. N-2214/K has been **Y** since the first of a month in the year before his father went in, and there is no column anywhere on the sheet that says what Y does. Forty-one rows are Y, thirty-one are N, eighteen are a dash, and **eleven of the eighteen dashes sit on rows that carry a hold.**
3. **A register of authorisations with eight columns, heads printed over the first six and nothing whatever over the seventh and the eighth, and no column for what the work was for.** The seventh and the eighth are the two this building had to name itself: *authorised by* and *work description*, **and those two names are ours and not the district's, and they are entered that way.** The seventh is a hand. The register **does not name months** — a day and a month as two bare numbers under a printed head with no word on it — **and the order date is spoken as *one and nine*, and a character may name a month in speech but nobody in Tarrow Fen may name the month on a district form, because the form does not carry one. That distinction is now load-bearing in Chapter 59 and a later batch must keep it.**
4. **A clerk's hand in nine entries out of ten in the seventh column, and a man's in the fifth.** The second half of the word *schedule* leans four degrees to the left of the first half and the tail of the *c* is doubled back. **A hand is not a name, a name is not a man, and a man is not a decision**, and the register is a transcript and **the original is the thing nobody produces and the applicant is not entitled to.**
5. **The hand is Aldren Vale's**, on a sheet of order paper for a hundred and forty rods of twelve inch, on a day in the eleventh month, and he made the W in *wire* the way he made the W in *weir*. **The order date on the register is the first of a month, in the year before the year of the shaft, seven months before it, and it is on the page as two bare numbers and a district form that does not name a month. The fieldbook's own figure is a year and eight months after the disappearance. Twenty-seven months apart, one hand, and nobody in Tarrow Fen has been asked which of the three things a hand can be.**
6. **The batch's one panel is spent on the wrong question and the batch says so out loud.** A bead goes against a wetted seed lot and reports where the lot was grown in four seconds; it cannot be laid against paper at all, and the question in that room for four days was a hand on a page. **Chapter 59 now states this on the page rather than leaving it for a later writer, so no batch inherits it as an unpaid debt, and no instrument in this district will read a page.**
7. **Three names in the seed house's own ledger that a reader of thirty-one years cannot say are wrong**, in a printed index of four hundred names that does not contain them, in a book anybody may copy, in a fen whose seed house has put the fieldbook and the use log on the middle of the table where anybody may ask to have either read. **A shilling a line is the market price of the thing that cannot be checked.**
8. **The half nobody has touched.** Nobody in this batch has seen the register itself, has asked what Y does, has asked who is in it, or has asked why a district would draw a line it maintains itself. **The answer is not in Lantern Reach and the batch's own argument is that the answer was never going to be in a place that keeps books on a table.**

### Clocks Volume 02 opens its second movement with, all of them live

| Clock | Where it stands at the end of Batch 0001 | What it owes |
|---|---|---|
| **The conditional charter** | **the term closes with the closing of the sowing; the closing of the sowing is the last day of the month after next; the charter expires on the twenty-ninth of that month — fifty-nine days from the first of a month — and there is no appeal anywhere in the four clauses** | **The volume's spine.** Ruben Ashe comes back at exactly that date and was told so in this holding's own book |
| **The season-end form** | answered on the first by the examiner, in the second wording, with **a full-draw load test recorded as a thing that did not happen, in a book the district may read, in her hand, under her name** | One box, two words, no third answer. **The ordinary way is a matter for the district** |
| **The C/4 records demand** | served on the Dryfoot Trust at Cray on the twenty-seventh, **fourteen days**, copy for the district file, and **a copy offered to any other party to the annex.** Eight days of slack on a cart that goes when carts go. **Tova Reed has written two paragraphs at the counting-house table and put them on the Thursday post, and the trust's answer has not been written** | The cooperative's records are now in a third party's hands because the cooperative's own annex said they would be, and a district asked |
| **The monthly report** | a charter condition, due on the first of every month, **nine lines and short, and nobody in the yard can say what the root changed** | Nia is not here and the work does not stop |
| **The leak** | **five-eighths of an inch, twice measured, in daylight, pump on; one sixteenth in thirteen days and no rate written, because the two ends of the two readings are different kinds of day.** Not mended, cause not established, into the housing and not into the pit | **It cannot be mended while the pump runs and the pump cannot stop until the Longneck is out. It is not a mystery and it is not a plot** |
| **The return main** | clause three, **to specification, forty-one rods of twelve, by a date the district has not fixed.** The rider asking it to fix the date and to say what the specification is **is unanswered** | The 399 hundredweight shortfall is carried forward and not discharged and no later chapter may quietly fill it |
| **Coll Renner's form** | promised in advance at the gate, **a month out, a form nobody in this district has seen** | He asked for the order of the work and was refused the book and given the refusal in a hundred and sixty words. He read it in the dark and called it a bad book and the first honest one anybody has put in front of him |
| **Ruben Ashe** | **named in the open book as returning at the end of the season** | Day 120 minus one |
| **Vesta Lund's index** | prints again this winter; a hundred and forty sheets going out; **two categories it does not print and one of them is a line held by nobody anybody knows of** | It does not know this fen has a branch in it |
| **The March's tin** | **forty-five days at risk on the fourth of a month, unmoved, on a bed at three and a half degrees** | The dry-land line is still lost |
| **The graft** | four willow sets alive and the anchor of a licensed act whose return line is **399 hundredweight short** and has been since the day it was written in a licensed engineer's hand | The starve is not visible from the top of the field and a licensed contractor has now said why this holding cannot afford itself |

## State after Volume 02 Batch 0002 (Chapters 60–68, days 65–84) — MOVEMENT 2

**Movement 2 delivered what it promised: refusing a buyer is not free, the fen's own book is the only thing in the world a stranger can check, and the same property is what makes it an invitation. The chapter ends on the district saying it will want the map next, and nobody in the room says the word.** Nothing below is closed.

**The day clock, and a repair.** The batch prompt's day-clock table is **off by one against its own weekday column**; the manuscript is dated from the locked weekday chain, so **days 65–84 are the fifth through the twenty-fourth and day 85 is unused and belongs to Chapters 69–76**, and **the fourteenth is day 74**. Every distance on the page was computed from day numbers. **The March's tin is *day minus nineteen*** (46 at the fifth, 65 at the twenty-fourth), anchored on three closed values that chain exactly. Full table, all distances, and the tin reconciliation are in `state/continuity.md` under *VOLUME 02 — BATCH 0002*.

### The new question for Volume 02, and how to keep it answerable

**Who altered the maps and the seed records enough to make a bridge between this fen and the dry Salt March legal — and who was paid for the alteration?** Still unanswered, still with the shape on the page and the content nowhere. **What this batch added is one line and no answer:** on the twenty-fourth a district officer of the district water office closed her file and said that **the district will want the map next, because a Crown seed-route map of this district is nine years old and there is a legend mark on it that this holding can now name.** Nobody in that room said the word. Tova Reed did not touch her case. Marek did not look at the sheet. That is the next movement's door and it has been opened by a structural act that is correct, timely and not an act by a man.

The eight things the batch put on the record, which a later writer can build Movement 3 on without reconstructing the manuscript:

1. **A district officer is coming for the map, and the reason is a nine-year-old Crown sheet and a legend mark this fen can name.** The claim on the map is now external. It stopped being a thing this holding wanted and became a thing the district requires.
2. **A district file is the only place in this district where the draw of a private charter can be recorded without anybody's consent**, and **eighty a day for six years is in it now, on a licensed man's own disclosure of his own breach**, and it will be read at the next renewal, variation, or objection. **The open book worked in the fen's favour for the first time and the fen did not want it.**
3. **The full-draw load test is ordered, on the district's order, at the next examination, with a licensed engineer present, and the paper is the district's.** Marek asked for it and will not have the first copy. **The next examination is the one at which the volume's first licensed witness will have to be somebody who is not Sera Quill.**
4. **The trade article exists and is technically accurate and names nobody**, and the defence anybody has is a book, and **a book only works if somebody reads it** — and on the twenty-first a stranger read it, all of it, with a pencil, and is going to write to a printer, and told the fen in advance in those words.
5. **The fen bought its enemy's printer for two shillings and put it on the middle of the table**, and the index's doubt about the Ashcombe oat has cost the March a sale of forty-five bags, entered in Tova Reed's own hand as the first line she has ever entered that says she was wrong about a thing and not about a number.
6. **A professional body turns out to be a market and not an office, and a man who is refused a method leaves a route on the table**, and the route names eleven farms on this fen road. **Coll Renner's form RW/3 will come back in a season with the line at the foot filled in by somebody else, and he does not know whether that is a threat or a market and asked for it to be entered that he could not tell from where he was standing.**
7. **The third of the three conditions in clause three is still unnamed, and it is now in a stranger's notebook two hundred miles from here.** **The first is the compost at four hundred hundredweight a month and the second is the return main to specification; the district has not printed the third and is not required to describe a condition in advance of the examination at which it is tested. Two state files said *the second*; the chapters and the prompt said *the third*; the third is right and both files are corrected.** A holding is under a condition it cannot read and cannot comply with, and that is a fact about a form and not about a conspiracy.
8. **The instrument reads a node and not a book, and the third of the three lines it gave at the head on the twelfth is in force: a node does not keep a list.** The fen cannot manufacture provenance out of the ground, and the eighty a day is a figure about a mouth and will never be a figure about a place. **This is the rule Movement 3 works against and it may not be quietly suspended.**

## State after Volume 02 Batch 0003 (Chapters 69–76, days 85–104) — MOVEMENT 3

**Movement 3 delivered what it promised: a device that works, an argument that the device is the problem, and a decision taken in a yard in front of a book that cost the fen the compost and cost Tova Reed the largest wrong of her career. The chapter ends with a letter from a woman who gave a name and no house, offering a shilling a copy of this holding's eleven years to anybody who has one to sell.** Nothing below is closed.

**The day clock.** The batch is **days 85–104, the twenty-fifth of one month through the fourteenth of the next.** Day 92, the second of the next month, falls inside Ch 72 and carries no chapter and no entry of its own. The weekday chain is anchored on day 61 = the first = a Thursday. **The charter expires on day 119, the twenty-ninth of the month after next, which is thirty-four days from this batch's first day and fifteen from its last.** The March's tin is *day minus nineteen* and stands at eighty-five days at risk on the fourteenth. Full table, every distance on the page, the tin reconciliation and the change to the dry-land line are in `state/continuity.md` under *VOLUME 02 — BATCH 0003*.

### The new question for Volume 02, and how to keep it answerable

**Who altered the maps and the seed records enough to make a bridge between a fen and the dry Salt March legal — and who was paid for the alteration?** Still unanswered. **This batch has added to the shape and has not added a crumb to the content, and the additions are deliberately the wrong size.** What is now on the record:

1. **A list of eleven holdings in a clerk's hand, under a heading in Tova Reed's own, dated the eleventh month two seasons back**, with no mark on it, no form behind it and no column in any book — because the trust's crop histories have eleven columns and the seventh is *source* and has never held anything but a place, and there is no column for cuttings. **The eleventh name is this fen.**
2. **A certificate of origin that is true.** The Marrowfield lot is nine parts carrier and one part the March's dry line, and the device reports the line, and the certificate says Salt March dry, and every word of it is correct. **A lot of one part in eleven is a legal sale and the trade has agreed to call it one, and the law has not been asked.**
3. **A shed at Marrowfield, a man in it, and a buyer who has never looked at a cutting sheet in twenty-two years because there has never been a column for one.** She has said in print and in a yard that she does not know, and she has paid a shilling for the fen's eleven years, and she intends to find out who the eleventh holding is.
4. **The largest wrong any of them has entered**, read out in a yard in daylight by the fen's own keeper, printed on the front page of a two-hundred-page record, and going to Cray: the fen copied its own name off a Crown list in a year it does not name, **and the name was written at Cray by the trust's steward in a season when an unestablished name would not have been sown and thirty-one households would otherwise have gone into the winter without a bean.**
5. **A map mark drawn nine years ago** that says a line is maintained and has no local holder, printed four hundred times and sold to a hundred and forty seed houses, **and a return to a district saying that the holder exists and is this holding.** Somebody in an office now has to decide what to do about a holder that appeared on a map that said there wasn't one. **That is the door into the next volume and into Movement 4, and it is a form and not a man.**
6. **The half nobody has touched.** Nobody in this batch has seen the Crown's map layer, has seen who signed for the ninth legend mark, has seen a payment, or has asked a district to produce anything it is not required to produce. **A person, a list of eleven names and a shed are not a map, a printer and a reason, and a later writer may not let them become one.**

### Carried forward from Volume 01 and still not closed

All eight Volume 01 threads stand unchanged. **The pressure pulse is logged eleven times and is undiagnosed; the third exchange requirement is still missing and now has a test date on the other side of the charter's death; the works schedule behind the certified page is still not produced and the applicant is not entitled to it and no villain may reverse that; the settlement with no seat is not named and the word *seat* does not appear anywhere in Chapters 69–76 and the long line is untouched; the collie is not interpreted and the weir animal does not return and Marek has still not walked the low quarter's own map and the copy of it is still on this holding's table unopened and entered as not of this holding; the sealed envelope is not opened; Nia does not come back and nobody may say she decided not to deliver the letter and the letter is now unopened and undiscussed across four batches; and Sera Quill's fourth refusal is not numbered, not explained, and is not to be asked about before Volume 03.** **The leak is a leak, five-eighths, unmoved under a full draw, unmended, undiagnosed, and no chapter may make it a mystery. The 399 hundredweight is carried forward and not discharged. The bypass is unbuilt and unfunded and Ivo Tarrant's four men do not come back.**

October is **two months back** throughout and is named nowhere. **No document in this batch carries a month name**, and the one figure that would have needed one — the cutting list — is dated *the eleventh month two seasons back*, which is how Tova Reed's own heading has read all along.
- **The fieldbook and the use log and the two hundred pages of the Ashcombe index are on the middle of the seed-house table together, and they stay there.** The fieldbook was not opened in this batch by anybody. **The fieldbook's three lines of the thirtieth of a month two months back were not quoted, and the Ch 63 panel's three lines were not, and this batch's own three lines are not in any document and Lissa Vale refused them twice out loud.** The use log records contacts and never readings, and it is now eleven lines.

- **Lissa Vale is not forgiven and is not thanked.** She refused a reading in nine lines, the book has a hole in it because of her, she put her hand up first and would not say which way, and she read out a keeper's confession because she is the reader and the reader reads. **Her refusal of the fourth of a month of Batch 0002 is hers, is not Sera Quill's, is not her own fourth, and is not numbered.**

- **No second demand for the register may be sent and none was, and no demand for the Crown's map layer may be sent, and none was.** The map went with a counter-mark and a statement, not with a claim.

## State after Volume 02 Batch 0004 (Chapters 77–88, days 105–129) — MOVEMENT 4

**Movement 4 delivered what it promised: a public schedule with two ends and a return, a measured capacity that is a fifth of what was announced and is on a door, a charter that expired correctly on a date nobody chose, and a first convoy that went half way and is still right. The chapter ends on a clerk's letter of four points, one of which says that a route over ground the district maintains is a class of use, and that terms will be sent. Nothing below is closed.**

**The day clock.** The batch is **days 105–129, the fifteenth of one month through the ninth of the next, crossing one month boundary after Chapter 84.** Day 104 was a Friday, which is Batch 0003's last day, and **the charter expired at midnight on day 119, the twenty-ninth, which is a Saturday and is Chapter 84 and is the batch's midpoint.** **The first of next month is a Monday, day 121, and the convoy moves on a Saturday, day 126, because a convoy moves when carts go.** The March's tin is *day minus nineteen* and stands at 110 days at risk on the ninth, unmoved, on a bed at three and a half degrees. **No month is named anywhere in Chapters 77–88 and no document in the batch carries one.** Full table, every distance on the page and the reconciliation of the count of the readings of the seventh column are in `state/continuity.md` under *VOLUME 02 — BATCH 0004*.

### The new question for Volume 02, and how to keep it answerable

**Who altered the maps and the seed records enough to make a bridge between a fen and the dry Salt March legal — and who was paid for the alteration?** Still unanswered. **This batch added the most consequential shape of it yet and no crumb of the content, and the addition is a Crown category rather than a person or a map.** What is now on the record:

1. **A name and a count at the far end.** **Wrenlow: eleven households, eleven plots in a ring about two hundred yards across, on the low dry, a well at the low end and in, no gate, no charter, no district holding number, and a keeper of thirty-nine named Wenna Roke who has never signed a paper in her life and cannot read a form.** The place had never been named in any book until this batch, and the batch names it once and then uses it.
2. **The Crown's own map and the Crown's own form agree with each other and that is the finding.** The seed-route sheet of nine years ago says the low dry is ground in district schedule. The district's own form of the nineteenth asks for a **six-digit district holding number** and a box for the name of a place, **and ground in district schedule is ground with no holding number, so the office could not fill in its own form and sent a letter instead.** A Crown map and a Crown form, both correct, both drawn by nobody in particular, together produce a place that cannot be asked about on paper. **That is the volume's argument in two documents and it needs no conspiracy.**
3. **A class of use.** A route carrying seed over ground the district carries in schedule is a use of that ground, and a use is a class of use, and classes of use carry terms, and terms will name a rate. **The district has named the category. The district has not named a rate and has not sent a term and does not say whether there will be a fee.** That is Movement 5's whole mechanism and it is now in the book in four correct paragraphs.
4. **A holder that appeared on a map of nine years ago is in a district file, in the district's book, and not in the Crown's map layer** — and the applicant is not entitled to the layer and has not asked for it. **The map is not amended on the statement of a holder and is revised on a cycle the office does not fix and cannot state.** The layer is now structurally out of reach for the third time and a later writer may not overturn it without a reason the reader can follow.
5. **A cutting sheet, written by a buyer who was told there is no column for it and put a third column on it anyway**, and **it has not been answered**, and a body with no charter has no method for answering it. **A missing column is now in the hands of both ends of the trade.**
6. **A seed-route bridge is a route with a return, and the return is the compost line, and the compost line is empty, and the measured capacity of the bridge is six crates a month under a declared thirty.** The whole of the volume's argument about a record and a return is now a sheet of paper on a door with a smaller figure under a larger one in the fen's own hand. **A bridge that is a fifth of what it was announced as is still a bridge, and the fen said so out loud, and a man who had been waiting all season for a reason to close the book was given one and could not use it.**
7. **The half nobody has touched.** Nobody in this batch has seen the Crown's map layer, has seen who signed for the ninth legend mark, has seen a payment, has seen a term, has seen a rate, or has asked a district to produce anything it is not required to produce. **A name, a keeper, a category of use, a cutting sheet and a five-parts-in-six road are not a map, a printer and a reason, and a later writer may not let them become one.**

### Clocks Volume 02 opens its last movement with, all of them live

| Clock | Where it stands at the end of Batch 0004 | What it owes |
|---|---|---|
| **The conditional charter** | **gone. It lapsed at midnight on the twenty-ninth, on a date nobody chose, with no appeal anywhere in the four clauses, and no officer obstructed it and none had to be there.** Seven practices are now kept by choice, by a vote of four hands to three in daylight with the book shut, and one condition was withdrawn from the motion and entered as a fact, and one practice turned out never to have been a condition at all | **Nothing may be reinstated, waived or referred, and nothing may stop being kept.** The volume's spine has fired |
| **The third of the three conditions in clause three** | **untested, unowned, unnamed, and its test date has been and gone.** The first examination held after the term ended was the seventh hour of the first of next month, it was held, and it was not described, because there was no officer and no form and nothing to appoint an examiner under | **The hardest thing in the volume and it is a form.** No chapter may name it, soften it, or have anybody guess at it, and the fen has decided not to write and ask |
| **The class of use** | **named by the district on the ninth, in four correct paragraphs, and no rate and no term has been sent.** The office says it will send terms in the ordinary course, that they will name a rate and a class of use, and that it asks for no fee now and says nothing about whether a fee will be asked for | **Movement 5's mechanism.** It is a category and not a villain, and a later chapter may not have arrived at it by any other road |
| **The Crown's map layer** | **not produced, not required, and the district has said in writing that the applicant is not entitled to it and has not asked for it.** The sheet is not amended on a holder's statement and the revision cycle is not fixed and cannot be stated | **Out of reach for the third time. No villain may reverse it** |

| **The C/4** | **discharged as a demand, not as a debt, in nine sheets with a schedule of four things the trust cannot produce, one of them a column.** No answer had been receivable by this holding at any point and there is still none, and the holding's own late answer to the March went on the Thursday cart with the first copy of the printing | Spent. The four things on the schedule are the volume's subject matter |

| **The dry end** | **Wrenlow, eleven households, a keeper of thirty-nine, a list on lining paper under a stone with no column for where anything came from, a refused record, a refused return, a returned set of terms, a kept sheet of forty-one words with a stone on it, six crates in eleven plots, and a schedule nailed to a post with no name on it** | **Movement 4's far end, named and now used.** A later chapter may not have her sign anything or hold a copy or answer on a record |
| **The printing** | **two hundred pages, forty copies, one sold in the yard, one to Marrowfield, one to Cray, one down the fen road, one on a post on the low dry, one read aloud to a carter's question about a column.** It cannot be recalled | The volume's answer, and it is irreversible, and a person at the far end of a route **cannot read it** |
| **The full-draw load test** | **performed, the figure in a district file, the holding not to have a copy.** It accounts for a gallon of nothing and says so in the fen's own hand | The number is true in front of somebody who can check it and explains nothing, and both of those are permanent |
| **The leak** | **five-eighths, twice measured, at the seventh hour, and still five-eighths at the seventh hour of the first of next month, measured by a woman who has no appointment to measure it, and the fen did not use the charter's expiry to stop measuring it** | Standing unsolved engineering, not a mystery, and no chapter may make the expiry a reason to stop |
| **The beat** | **logged twelve times, on the first of next month, cause not established, licensed person present none, and nothing written underneath it** | Undiagnosed. The slowest thing in the book |
| **The 399 hundredweight** | **carried forward and not discharged for the third month running. Nothing put in, none available. Seventy-nine pounds sixteen at four shillings.** And entered in capitals: **no person in this yard may call the return a solution to it** | The compost is still short and the graft is still fed about a twentieth and the cost is not refunded |
| **The certificate market and the collar market** | **two hundred certificates, thirty-one used, four shillings a lot, a keeper line the device cannot fill in and the seller fills; and four collars in a Fennmere yard, a fifth man of unknown origin, twelve pounds for a Sunday refused in writing, and no form anywhere in the district for any of it** | Structural and unfixable in this district, and no villain may be inserted to fix it |
| **Ottoline Fray's cutting sheet** | **received on the fourth of next month, read aloud twice, entered, and unanswered.** A trade form that does not exist with a third column on it that a buyer put there herself | **A missing column in the trade's own hand, and the fen has no method and no standing and has entered that it has neither** |
| **Coll Renner's last page** | **two hundred words and Tova Reed's two sentences, set in the fen's printing, and he has read them twice and says the last page is the best page of the two hundred** | In four counties and cannot be recalled |
| **Vesta Lund's index** | **still on the middle of the table, two hundred pages, open at a column that does not name this fen, and the man of about fifty's reason for voting against keeping the book is now on the page: how a body is known is not how it is honest, it is how big it is** | Four hundred smallholders buy it this winter |
| **The March's tin** | **110 days at risk on the ninth, unmoved, on a bed at three and a half degrees, with both undertakings standing, and the dry line is now four inches high in six trays on this fen and the sowing closed on the twenty-ninth** | The trust's seed is being held for a season by choice, and it is a return, and it goes to Cray |

## State after Volume 03 Batch 0001 (Chapters 99–108, days 151–170) — MOVEMENT 1

**Volume 03 opened off the fen and paid for it. The sowing closed, the road was rewritten on one page, a woman walked nine days down a plateau with a crate of last season's pears, a row of eleven dressed cuts was dated to this season with a rule, the first draw in the book cost a man his taste and a plateau its well, an office arrived with a form that takes a record away by listing it, and a dead man's work order with the word *necessary* at the foot of it was copied into four hands before anybody could declare it part three. Nothing below is closed.**

### The new question for Volume 03, and how to keep it answerable

**The question from Volume 02 — who changed the maps and who was paid — is still unanswered and was not touched in this batch.** What this batch added is one more shape and still no content, and the shape is the sharpest thing in the book so far.

1. **A sequence and not a scar.** Taken, dressed, closed, left alone long enough to close, taken again. Eleven times, on one row, in one week, **this season**, by a method, with a smooth face and a bevel on one side only. **That is not damage. That is an order of operations, and an order of operations has an author.**
2. **A tool that is not in the district.** No chisel and no draw-knife on the hire sheet at the change of horses or on the district's own sheet at the foot of the plateau. A licensed man has seen that bevel once and would rather not say whose it was in a barn. **He was not asked again.**
3. **Two readings of the same eleven, and neither wins.** A man in a hurry against a season, or a list made before he started. **The fen's book cannot hold two findings under one line and entered none, and this is the deepest thing the volume has found and it is a disagreement rather than a fact.**
4. **A work order in Aldren Vale's hand**, district paper, a sheet of three, an office named at the head and not explained, a direction to a named node in the year of the shaft, **the word *necessary*, no reason on the page**, and an instruction to a clerk not to enter it against the node. **Marek cannot find out whether his father was right and has entered that he is not going to be able to.**
5. **An order that is not a reading and not a result.** *A direction given to another person is a record in which somebody is told what to do by somebody who was not the one who did the work.* **The part three condition takes a record away by listing it, and the way out of the form is the form.**
6. **The half nobody has touched.** Nobody in this batch has seen the Crown's map layer, has seen who signed for the ninth legend mark, has seen a payment, has seen the office named at the head of the work order, has heard the office explained, or has asked a records officer a question she did not answer first. **Eleven cuts, a bevel, a word, a form and a hand are not an office and not a reason, and a later writer may not let them become one.**
7. **An ordinary grief, correctly held.** A father is not a piece of paper. A body that cannot tell those two things apart will be told what it wants by the first man who comes up a road with a bag, and Tova Reed said that in a barn in front of nine people.
