### Carried forward unchanged

**All eight Volume 01 threads stand, plus the whole of Volume 02's shape.** The pressure pulse is logged fourteen times and undiagnosed and nobody has named it. The third exchange requirement is still missing with a test date that has gone. The works schedule behind the one certified page is not produced and the applicant is not entitled to it. The settlement with no seat is not named, the word *seat* does not appear in Chapters 99–108, and the Thornwild long line is untouched. The collie is not interpreted, the weir animal does not return, and Marek has still not walked the low quarter's own map. The envelope is sealed. **Nia's cart has been past the gate twice in a season and has not stopped, and nobody in that yard has asked why; her letter is sealed in a shut drawer, unopened and undiscussed, now across six batches and about a hundred and twenty days of season.** Sera Quill's fourth refusal is unnumbered and unasked and **was implied twice in this batch and only twice, and nothing more was spent.** The leak is a leak and is not mended. The 399 hundredweight is carried and not discharged and is now worse. The bypass is unbuilt and unfunded and Ivo Tarrant's four men have not come back. The class of use, the toll, the refusal clause, the arrangement, the sixty pounds ten, the two letters, the envelope and the cutting sheet are closed facts a later chapter may not undo. **The levy base is sixteen households and the steward's tenure is eleven years.**

### What Volume 03 Batch 0001 adds to the prohibitions

- **No Crown Engine as a working system, no Chancellor Iona Vey, no False Season, no history lecture.** The Quiet Break is not named. **The Continuity Office is not named on the page:** an office is named at the head of a column on a work order form and is not explained, and a records officer gives her name once and is entered by the clerk. **The name at the head of a column is not a history and a later chapter may not turn it into one.**
- **No month is named anywhere in Chapters 99–108** and no document in the batch carries one. October is five months back by day 151 and is named nowhere. The only month-shaped figures are the sixth of a month four months back (day 36, the picking) and the eleventh of a month two months back (day 101, the buyer's paper). *March* is not used at all in this batch.
- **The fieldbook, the use log and the Ashcombe index are not on one table in this batch** — the fen is on a plateau. **The fieldbook was opened once, in Ch 108, and is closed for the season. The Ch 101 panel's three lines are not quoted in any document, on any form, in any letter, or in any copy, and no character quotes them as authority.**
- **There is still no use log for the fieldbook, there never has been, and no later chapter may supply one, propose one, or thank anybody for the absence.** The use log is **thirteen** lines and records contacts and never readings.
- **Marek cannot read deep memory in this volume and no chapter may have him do it.** No vision, no father's voice, no archive. The sequence in the scarroot is read by counting healed cuts and dating them with a rule. **A later batch may not have him see his father, and may not have the fieldbook speak.**
- **Nothing romantic has happened and nothing may lean that way.** The milestone for this range is that they choose not to keep private records, and it is carried as a clause in a fen document and a refusal in a yard, not as a scene between two people.
- **Lissa Vale is not forgiven and is not thanked.** She opens her husband's book, she enters a second reason that is the true one, she signs a copy in a stranger's yard, and she is thanked for none of it. **She was never asked to open the fieldbook and no one asked her.**
- **Kellan Rusk is not in this batch and the words *became* and *redeemed* are both prohibited for him.**
- **No new named member of the cooperative.** Merrid Ollvar and Sabin Roell are the batch's two new named people and **neither is a member of anything.**

### Phase handoff, Volume 03 Batch 0001 → Batch 0002

- Chapters 99–108 are in `workspace/volume-03/batch-0001/` and summarized one compact entry per chapter in `state/chapter-summaries.md`. The volume's outline is `outline/volume-03.md`; the batch's governing prompt, which carries the ten cards, is `workspace/volume-03/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`.
- The batch's day clock, every distance on the page, the one day shared between Ch 106 and Ch 107, its new numbers, forms, documents, places and people, the twenty-second through twenty-sixth section-nine notes, the Ch 101 panel, the thirteenth use-log line, its three proposed canon changes and its stage accounting are all in `state/continuity.md` under *VOLUME 03 — BATCH 0001, CHAPTERS 99–108, MOVEMENT 1*.
- **Length: 28,582 words across ten chapters by `wc -w` over the chapter files including their chapter headings, and 2,620–3,119 per chapter**, after the Batch 0001 fix pass and a later day-naming pass, both itemised in `reviews/volume-03-batch-0001.findings.md`; it was 28,570 after the first of them, and 28,402 and 2,599–3,117 before that. Inside the 27,000–30,000 band and inside 2,550–3,120 at every chapter. **Ch 107 at 3,113 and Ch 108 at 3,119 are the two near a ceiling and a later writer must not lengthen either.** Ch 108 was drafted at 3,937 and was cut to 3,102 by trimming restatement the chapter had already made and not by cutting a scene, a document, a reading, a copy or a cost; Ch 101, 105 and 106 were topped up because they came in under the floor, and **what was added to them is scene, not summary.**
- **Staging was moved on purpose and the batch is not the seed-house table.** Ch 99, 100, 101 and 102 are at Lantern Reach. **Ch 103, 104, 105, 106, 107 and 108 are on a plateau in the Glassward, four hundred miles off the fen, in a walled yard and a stone barn.** **Ch 108 is the first chapter in forty-nine to open at Lantern Reach and be in a barn on a plateau in the same hour**, because the empty-yard reading and the fieldbook are the same day and the fen reads its ledger aloud wherever it is.
- **Four dates and two counts were settled by the fix pass and are now fixed, not open, and the next writer may not re-date them:** the day on the buying paper is **the seventh of next month, day 187**, which is the first day of Ch 117; the cuts on the third row are **one season old, this season, and the row carries eleven of them of which nine were read**; the links are numbered **one is the dead graft of the spring, two is the collar of the second tree in the row, three is the scion of the twelfth, four fails**, so the fen is two links down after Ch 105; **Merrid Ollvar's rule for the well is the fourth hour on the sixteenth of every month** and the figure of the fourteenth was an extra reading she gave because Marek Vale asked; the pears were picked on **the sixteenth of a month three months back**, which is eleven weeks before the gate on the third; and the fieldbook was last out of the press on **the twenty-eighth of a month two months back, day 118** and was not opened then either. **Four inherited readings are now carried as day numbers rather than as names, because a name changes at the month turn and a day number does not: the well is nine feet on day 136, five feet and a half on day 164, five feet five inches on day 166 and five feet four inches on day 170, which is three and a half feet down and entered against the draw and not the weather.** In Chapters 109–113 those are the sixteenth of last month, the fourteenth, the sixteenth and the twentieth of this month; in Chapters 114–118 all four are of last month except day 136, which is a month two months back. **Batch 0002 may not re-date any of the five and may not convert one of them to a name without knowing which side of Chapter 114 the scene is on.**
- **The next phase writes Chapters 109–118, Movement 2, *The Thing In The Yard*, and the prompt for it is `workspace/volume-03/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`.** It opens on **day 171, the twenty-first of this month, a Tuesday.** **The month does not turn at the start of this batch: day 180 is the thirtieth of this month and day 181 is the first of next month, so the turn falls between Ch 113 and Ch 114, and the monthly business is in Ch 114.** The four days down the plateau road fall on the twenty-seventh through the thirtieth and Batch 0002 must decide where the party is on each day of that window; the going is nine days up and four down and **no batch may quietly make it shorter.**
- **Batch 0002's first job is the leak, and its premise has to be stated correctly.** **Gael Senn measures the leak, at the seventh hour, twice, with two rules, the second of them the one that reads smaller on that corner, pump on, into the housing and not into the pit — and it is not a monthly figure and she did not go up the road. She stayed.** **The last entry in the book about it is the seventh hour of day 138, which is the eighteenth of last month in Chapters 109–113 and a month two months back in Chapters 114–118, and the two before it are day 135 and day 121, all three in Volume 02 Chapters 91 and 93. So the figure in the book is thirty-three days old when the batch opens.** **It did not stop because the party went up: eighteen days of the gap are days the whole fen was standing in that yard, and the yard was never empty — what the road took out was the number of people a figure could be said to in front of.** The gap cannot be dated at all, because a practice that was never written down leaves no account of itself, and **that is the better finding than the one this bullet used to carry**: the fen can show that the practice had stopped and not show when. Ch 109 measures it in a yard with three people in it; Ch 114 measures it again in a full yard, in public, with a reader appointed and the count of mornings read out with it, in the forty-third day, and it is five-eighths both times and it does not move. **A batch may not restate the gap as fourteen days, may not give the road as its cause, and may not say the yard was empty.**

## State after Volume 03 Batch 0002 (Chapters 109–118, days 171–190) — MOVEMENT 2

**Volume 03's second movement opened a family's stack, dated the wood in it, found a hand-made graft in a heap of waste, filled in a form that takes a record away by listing it, watched an office answer it correctly and immovably, satisfied a rule about a stop without discovering what comes after a stop, let four houses on a plateau work out their own water, sold a season's fruit to a man who had never been told the truth about it, and then made a copy of a dead man's work in a shape that no schedule has a part for. Nothing below is closed.**

### The new question for Volume 03, and how to keep it answerable

**The question from Volume 01 — who changed the maps and who was paid — is still unanswered and was not touched in this batch.** What this batch added is the first object in three volumes that a man made **to keep** and put where nobody would look at it, and a date for it, and no name.

1. **A maintenance sequence in the waste.** Outside first, then the wet, then the core, because a core cut live bleeds on the press and the bale goes at the seam. Four times in twenty-eight years, seven years, seven years and six years apart, the last about six years back, with bare years between. **That is an order of operations and it has a rotation in it, and nobody has ever seen that rotation anywhere else in this work.**
2. **Three living grafts in a rootwood press** — cut to cut, fitted, bound in bast, put in with the waste, on a shelf with no nursery in it, by a family that has never signed a piece of paper. **A graft in a press is nobody's hobby. A licensed man cannot read intent in wood. Both of those are on the page and neither is a conclusion.**
3. **The same tool in two places.** A single bevel, one edge, the same edge, in eleven pear limbs this season and in sixty-one bales of waste, and no chisel and no draw-knife and no shoulder-blade on the district's hire sheet at either end of the road. **The tool is the strongest thread in this work and it is still unaccounted for, and a licensed man has seen that bevel once and would rather not say whose it was in a barn, and was not asked again.**
4. **The work order is now in a class of record of its own.** It is a direction to a clerk and the fen has built a document out of the fact of the direction without reproducing the direction, and the fen knows that is the same trick as the refusal clause and has entered that it is the fourth time.
5. **The question, asked once, in a barn, and not answered.** *He made a list before he started, and the list is not on anything, and nobody knows whether it is still going on, or it is not. Which is it.* **A later batch may not answer it, may not have a character answer it, and may not have a character say that it is still going on.** A person may say what the evidence is and what it is not.
6. **The half nobody has touched.** Nobody has seen the office named at the head of the sheet explained. Nobody has heard the buyer say anything about the ground. Nobody has asked the man at the second house anything. Nobody has opened a bale that was not opened. **Twenty-eight layers, four bands, three grafts, a bevel and a word are not an office and not a reason, and a later writer may not let them become one.**
7. **The engineer's own gap.** He wrote nine lines on the seventh of next month that nobody asked for, sent them nine days up a road, and they will arrive after the decision they were written for. **He is a man who answers when nobody asks and is not read. That is a character fact and not a plot one, and Movement 3 is the volume in which he is going to be asked.**

| **The rate** | one shilling a month on a declared thirty, measured six under it, eightpence entered and not claimed | continues |

### Carried forward unchanged

**All eight Volume 01 threads stand, plus the whole of Volume 02's shape.** The pressure pulse is logged fifteen times and undiagnosed and nobody has named it. The third exchange requirement is still missing with a test date that has gone. The works schedule behind the one certified page is not produced and the applicant is not entitled to it. The settlement with no seat is not named, the word *seat* does not appear in Chapters 109–118, and the Thornwild long line is untouched. The collie is not interpreted, the weir animal does not return, and Marek has still not walked the low quarter's own map. The envelope is sealed. **Nia's cart has not been mentioned in this batch and nobody has asked why; her letter is sealed in a shut drawer, unopened and undiscussed, now across seven batches and about a hundred and thirty days of season.** Sera Quill's fourth refusal is unnumbered and unasked. The bypass is unbuilt and unfunded and Ivo Tarrant's four men have not come back. The class of use, the toll, the refusal clause, the arrangement, the two letters, the envelope and the cutting sheet are closed facts a later chapter may not undo. **The levy base is sixteen households and the steward's tenure is eleven years, and the steward's book now has one page in another hand in it and that is entered.**

### What Volume 03 Batch 0002 adds to the prohibitions

- **No Crown Engine as a working system, no Chancellor Iona Vey, no False Season, no history lecture.** The Quiet Break is not named. **The Continuity Office is not named on the page:** an office is named at the head of a sheet that is described in Ch 118's sequence and is not reproduced and is not explained.
- **No month is named anywhere in Chapters 109–118** and no document in the batch carries one. October is five months back by day 171 and is named nowhere. *March* is not used as a month; the only capitalized *March* is *the March's tin*.
- **The word *seat* does not appear.** The Fieldbook panel is not spent in this batch and the three lines from Ch 101 are not quoted in any document, on any form, in any letter, or in any copy.
- **There is still no use log for the fieldbook, there never has been, and no later chapter may supply one, propose one, or thank anybody for the absence.** The use log is **thirteen** lines and records contacts and never readings.
- **Marek cannot read deep memory in this volume and no chapter has had him do it.** No vision, no father's voice, no archive. The maintenance sequence is read by **counting layers and dating them with a rule and a lens**, which is engineering. **A later batch may not have him see his father, may not have the fieldbook speak, and may not have a character's intent read out of wood by a licensed man — he says on the page that he cannot read intent in wood.**
- **Nothing romantic has happened and nothing may lean that way.** The asking in Ch 115 is asked on behalf of the body in front of fourteen people and is not a scene between two people. The milestone for this range is that they choose not to keep private records, and it is carried as a classification argument about a document and a refusal about a book, and in Ch 117 as a sentence he wrote that nobody asked for and that arrives too late.
- **Lissa Vale is not forgiven and is not thanked.** She is right about the stack and pays for being right in public, she is refused nothing and thanks nobody, she says *no* to the fieldbook in one word with a third reason after it, and she is thanked for none of it.
- **Kellan Rusk is not in this batch and the words *became* and *redeemed* are both prohibited for him.**
- **No new named member of the cooperative.** Merrid Ollvar, Sabin Roell and Perrin Osse are named and **none of them is a member of anything.** The man at the second house is unnamed and is held by function.
- **The man at the second house is not angry yet and is not to be made angry, and the ninth day he named is the fifteenth of next month, day 195, which is Batch 0003's business and which Batch 0003 must honour.**

### Phase handoff, Volume 03 Batch 0002 → Batch 0003

- The batch's day clock, the month turn between Ch 113 and Ch 114, the two naming tables, its new numbers, forms, documents, places and people, the twenty-seventh through thirty-first section-nine notes, the panel decision, its two recorded deviations from its own card and its three proposed canon changes are all in `state/continuity.md` under *VOLUME 03 — BATCH 0002, CHAPTERS 109–118, MOVEMENT 2*.
- **Length: 28,816 words across ten chapters by `wc -w` over the chapter files including their chapter headings, and 2,647–3,119 per chapter.** Inside the 27,000–30,000 band and inside 2,550–3,120 at every chapter. **Ch 114 at 3,099 and Ch 118 at 3,119 are the two near a ceiling and a later writer must not lengthen either.** Ch 118 was drafted at 3,528 and was cut to 3,119 by trimming restatement the chapter had already made — the same treatment Ch 108 got in Batch 0001 — and not by cutting a scene, a document, a reading, a copy or a cost.
- **Staging.** Chs 109 and 112 are at Lantern Reach and the fen road. Chs 110 and 111 are in the walled yard at Cauldron Reach, four hundred miles off. Chs 113 to 118 are at Lantern Reach, with the buyer's day on the shelf in Ch 117 and the household's business on the shelf carried by a paid messenger in Ch 116, and **Ch 114's second half is at a counter nine miles off at Fennmere.** **No character appears on the shelf after the thirtieth who could not have got there: the road is nine days up and four down, the party came off it on the thirtieth, and the fen stayed.**
- **Two dates and two counts are settled by this batch and are now fixed, not open, and the next writer may not re-date them:** the sound wood in the stack is **twenty-eight years old, in four bands at intervals of seven years, seven years and six years, the last of them about six years back**; the well at Cauldron Reach is **five feet and two inches on the thirtieth of this month, three feet and ten inches against the sixteenth of last month**, with the draw's three and a half feet and the other half inch entered separately; the **fourth copy is a sequence** and is not classified by anybody; and the **man at the second house's ninth day is the fifteenth of next month, day 195.** The inherited readings still travel as day numbers and not as names — the well is nine feet on day 136, five and a half on 164, five feet five inches on 166 and five feet four inches on 170, and **five feet and two inches on day 180, which is new and is the fifth reading**; the leak's three are 121, 135 and 138, and it is measured on days 171 and 181. **A day number does not move at the month turn and a name does.**
- **The next phase writes Chapters 119–126, Movement 3, *What Sera Took Out*, and the prompt for it is to be drafted by the phase that reviews this one.** **It is Sera Quill's volume and it is the confession.** It opens on **day 191, the eleventh of next month, a Monday.** **Day 195 is the fifteenth of next month and it is the man at the second house's ninth day, and Batch 0003 must honour it in the first third of its range and must not have him angry before it.** The card states its own day clock and its own naming table, because the Batch 0001 and Batch 0002 passes both found dating errors in a queued prompt rather than in the prose.
- **The queued prompt takes ten chapters, 119–128, and days 191–210, and it states why: the volume outline's batch map gives 119–126 and skips 127 and 128, which leaves 99–147 two chapters short, and 191–210 is exactly the eleventh through the thirtieth of the seventh month.**
- **Batch 0003 must not spend a word of anything the confession has not earned, and the confession is not a rescue.** Sera Quill comes to Lantern Reach on the fen road and does not charge a fee; she admits what she did nineteen years ago; she gives Marek the relay fragment and nothing else; the sealed compensation record stays sealed. **The volume outline says she comes up the plateau road, and the staging decided by Batch 0002 makes that impossible inside ten chapters — the party came off that road on the thirtieth of last month and the fen is four hundred miles from the shelf, and nine days up and four down would be eighteen of the batch's twenty days. The confession is given in the fen's own yard, which is the whole argument of this manuscript.** **Sera's fourth refusal is unnumbered and may not be numbered, and it is spent in this volume and not before it.**

## State after Volume 03 Batch 0003 (Chapters 119–128, days 191–210) — MOVEMENT 3

**Volume 03's third movement brought a woman down a fen road without a fee, took her refusal of a document, held a statement it cannot corroborate, took a piece of a machine nobody can copy, and changed the volume's question from *is the telling still going on* to *whose order is it*, which is worse. A man came off a plateau road to be answered about a bucket, was answered, and did not accept the answer. The month closed with its own figures and no compost line. Nothing below is closed.**

### The new question for Volume 03, and how to keep it answerable

**The question from Volume 01 — who changed the maps and who was paid — is still unanswered and was not touched in this batch.** What this batch added is the first person in three volumes who has admitted a thing about the network in her own hand, and the admission is about records, and the record that would prove it is sealed and she will not open it and the fen cannot ask.

1. **A statement with nothing behind it.** Nine lines, read out whole in a room, entered, read back, and **the fen has entered in its own book that it holds a statement it cannot corroborate, and why: no paper, so no office can check it; a reading is not a witness, so no licensed man can check it; and she cannot check it either.** A later batch may not find a document.
2. **A piece of a machine that cannot be copied.** Four attempts in a yard, all refused, all entered. **It is in the engineer of record's keeping. Its description is in a drawer in the public room and with a person who is not named in this body's book and may not be named in it, and a copy held by a person nobody can name is not a copy anybody can find.**
3. **A decision that went right, in a woman's memory, with no table behind it.** The ground pruned hardest came back and the ground left alone did not. **She kept nothing, the figures were asked for, and they have not come, and it is entered as an account and not a finding.**
4. **The question has changed and may not change back.** *Is the telling still going on* was struck out of his own mouth in front of witnesses. **The question is now *whose order is it*, and the fen has entered that it is worse, and nobody in the batch answered either version.**
5. **A wall on both sides.** A record nobody can produce cannot corroborate a statement and cannot be used to attack one. **The fen has now built three of these in three weeks — the part three condition, the shape the schedule has no part for, and the sealed record — and has entered the count.**
6. **A cost that grows rather than one that is held.** She is the only living person who knows what is in the sealed record, on the reader's word and not on hers, and **the fen wanted a thing it could find in nine years' time and has decided it would rather have one it cannot.** The same sentence is the shape of the fragment's second copy and of the seedwright's own unnumbered thing.
7. **The half nobody has touched.** Nobody has seen the office at the head of the sheet explained. Nobody has opened the fieldbook and nobody will ask again. Nobody has asked a records officer a question she did not answer first. Nobody has looked inside the ninety pounds ten. **A statement, a fragment, a word, a sealed record and a table nobody kept are not an office and not a reason, and Movement 4 may not let them become one.**
8. **The engineer's own gap, narrowed and not closed.** He answered a man in a yard on the fifteenth with four entries and no apology, and he wrote on the eighteenth of last month that he is not going to be able to find out whether his father was right. **A woman has now looked at his arm and said nothing, and the fen has entered that she said nothing and not why, and the two accounts of that arm still do not agree.**

| **The compost line** | sixth month: **four hundred and fifty-two hundredweight, ninety pounds eight**, six months not discharged. **The seventh month's line falls on the first of next month, day 211, and the figure is not in this book and this holding does not know what it is** | **the first scene of Batch 0004, and it is a figure nobody can say** |

A person may say what the evidence is and what it is not** |

| **Sixty pounds ten** | not paid, not remitted, not reduced, file open, reservation of day 84 standing | **not touched in this batch and not softened because nobody went looking** |
| **The third condition** | untested, unowned, unnamed, test date gone, the fen has decided not to write and ask | still undescribed, still not to be guessed at |

### Carried forward unchanged

**All eight Volume 01 threads stand, plus the whole of Volume 02's shape and the whole of Volume 03's first two movements.** The pressure pulse is logged fifteen times and undiagnosed and nobody has named it. The third exchange requirement is still missing with a test date that has gone. The works schedule behind the one certified page is not produced and the applicant is not entitled to it. The settlement with no seat is not named, the word *seat* does not appear in Chapters 119–128, and the Thornwild long line is untouched. The collie is not interpreted, the weir animal does not return, and Marek has still not walked the low quarter's own map. The envelope is sealed. **Nia's cart has not been mentioned in this batch and nobody has asked why; her letter is sealed in a shut drawer, unopened and undiscussed, now across eight batches and about a hundred and fifty days of season.** **Sera Quill's fourth refusal is spent, unnumbered, and it may not be numbered.** The bypass is unbuilt and unfunded and Ivo Tarrant's four men have not come back. The class of use, the toll, the refusal clause, the arrangement, the two letters, the envelope and the cutting sheet are closed facts a later chapter may not undo. **The levy base is sixteen households and the steward's tenure is eleven years, and the steward's book has one page in another hand in it and that is entered.**

### What Volume 03 Batch 0003 adds to the prohibitions

- **No Crown Engine as a working system, no Chancellor Iona Vey, no False Season, and no history lecture.** **The Continuity Office is named once, on paper only, in the statement of the seventeenth, as the office at the head of the district's index of relay designs, and it is not explained and it is not in narration.** The Quiet Break appears once, in the ninth line of that statement, **as a refusal carrying an age and not as a dated refusal: a hundred and twenty-one years old, and she was not born. A number, an age, no date, no region and no history, and none of those four may be put back in.** **A later chapter may not explain the office, expand it, or put it in a document in any other sense.**
- **Brinewake is RESERVED, not prohibited, and this is now a decision rather than an absence.** The name is canon in `bible/terminology.md`, `bible/world.md`, `bible/characters.md`, `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` and `outline/volume-03.md`, it is not on the page anywhere in Chapters 99–128, **and `outline/ending.md` gives Volume 04 a restitution beat built on it.** **The binding arrangement: no chapter of Volume 03 puts the name in anybody's mouth or in any document of any kind, and Volume 04 has it out of Sera Quill's own mouth.** It may not come from an office, a form, a district index, a log, or a glossary, and it may not be guessed at. **The reason it is worth holding to the end of the volume is that the name is the largest instance of the volume's own defect — a fact that lives in one uncorroborated memory, checkable by nobody including the woman who holds it, or in a sealed compensation record that does not open in this volume. The bible itself says Movement 3 must not ask her about it, and she has not offered it.** See the Batch 0003 review pass in `state/continuity.md`.
- **No month is named anywhere in Chapters 119–128** and no document in the batch carries one. October is seven months back by day 191 and is named nowhere. *March* is not a month; the only capitalized *March* is *the March's tin*.
- **The word *seat* does not appear.** The Fieldbook panel is not spent in this batch and the three lines from Ch 101 are not quoted in any document, on any form, in any letter, or in any copy.
- **There is still no use log for the fieldbook, there never has been, and no later chapter may supply one, propose one, or thank anybody for the absence.** The use log is **thirteen** lines and records contacts and never readings. **A second asking for the fieldbook is not a beat: the one asking in this season was refused in one word in front of eight people on the sixth of this month and the work order reached the table in Ch 126 without the book.**
- **Marek cannot read deep memory in this volume and no chapter in this batch had him do it.** No vision, no father's voice, no archive. The relay is a piece of metal read with a clip and a lens, and **a licensed man said out loud in a yard that he is not going to touch the bead and that he cannot read intent in metal.** A later batch may not have him see his father, may not have the fieldbook speak, and may not have anybody read intent out of the fragment.
- **Nothing romantic has happened and nothing may lean that way.** There is no scene between Marek Vale and Sera Quill, no scene between Marek and Tova, and no reconciliation between a son and a dead man's colleague. The milestone for this range — **that they choose not to keep private records** — was carried as a rule about custody, as a refusal about a name, and as a woman's refusal in a room with nine people in it, and not as a scene between two people.
- **Lissa Vale is not forgiven and is not thanked.** She paid a fee she refused, said out loud that a body which does a thing twice has not changed, copied four lines out of a dead man's book, and stood at a bench for four minutes while nobody looked at her. **Tova Reed is not thanked and refused to make anything of anything.**
- **Kellan Rusk is not in this batch, and the words *became* and *redeemed* are prohibited for him only.** *Became* is on the page once, in Ch 127, in a sentence about a compliment turning into a standing, and *redeemed* does not appear in the batch. **The lock is his; it is not a ban on the words in anybody else's mouth and a later pass may not treat it as one.**
- **The hired man's rate is now a standing of the compost line and a later batch may not enter a cheaper one.** The road is **four days**, a round trip is **eight days of it**, and a man at **four shillings a day** is **thirty-two shillings**, entered **four on the day he is hired and the rest when he is paid off.** Batch 0003's man was hired on the **twelfth of the seventh month** and paid off on the **sixteenth**, and the fen entered that it did not have the thirty-two shillings and took them out of that line anyway. **The eight-shilling whole hire that three Batch 0004 cards carried is an impossibility and has been corrected there.**
- **No new named member of the cooperative.** Sera Quill is a named person and **is not a member of anything and was not asked and may not be made one.** The person holding the second copy of the fragment's description **has no name in this body's book and may not be given one.** The man of about fifty and the man of about seventy remain held by function and were not given names. The crew is unchanged: Ilsa Kern, Jonas Byre, Anneke Roos, Gael Senn, Lissa Vale, Dunmar Fitch, Marek Vale, plus Tova Reed and Coll Renner.

- Chapters 119–128 are in `workspace/volume-03/batch-0003/` and summarized one compact entry per chapter in `state/chapter-summaries.md`. The volume's outline is `outline/volume-03.md`; the batch's governing prompt, which carries the ten cards, is `workspace/volume-03/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`.
- The batch's day clock, its single naming table, its new numbers, forms, documents, places and people, the thirty-second through thirty-sixth section-nine notes, the panel decision, **its three recorded deviations from its own card**, its three proposed canon changes **and the full text of the twenty-six-finding review pass that repaired it** are all in `state/continuity.md` under *VOLUME 03 — BATCH 0003, CHAPTERS 119–128, MOVEMENT 3*.
- **Length: 29,496 words across ten chapters by `wc -w` over the chapter files including their chapter headings, and 2,720–3,119 per chapter.** Inside the 27,000–30,000 band and inside 2,550–3,120 at every chapter. **Ch 123 at 3,119 and Ch 119 at 3,118 are the two near a ceiling and a later writer must not lengthen either.**

The man at the second house is in the fen from day 195 to day 210 and nowhere else. No character is on the shelf and back at the fen inside ten days.** **The hired man is the one journey that crosses in this batch and he is the exception the batch is built on: hired on the twelfth, up the four-day road, and paid off on the sixteenth, and the road is four days and not nine, and a later writer may not make it nine and may not make his hire cost less than thirty-two shillings.**
- **Three dates and two counts are settled by this batch and are now fixed, not open, and the next writer may not re-date them:** the cost of the statement is **six days** of a fortnight's living and not four, **and the six days she was here over the fortnight were entered on the thirtieth at one shilling and sevenpence, with the reason they were not entered on the twenty-fourth**; **the man at the second house's ninth day is the fifteenth of this month, day 195**; and the man of about seventy is **brought out fourth on the fourteenth, fifth on the twenty-first, sixth on the twenty-fourth and seventh on the twenty-seventh, and his three standing sentences were given on the fifth of a month two months back, at a pump, and on the twenty-seventh, and not within a fortnight.** The well at Cauldron Reach is **five feet and three inches on the sixteenth of this month** and the shortfall is **three feet and nine inches, three and a half against a man and half an inch against the ground**; and the **count of the readings of the seventh column is twenty-five on the sixteenth and twenty-six on the thirtieth, fourteen days apart.** The boards are **day minus sixty-six and one hundred and forty-four on the thirtieth**, the tin is **day minus nineteen and one hundred and ninety-one on the thirtieth**, and the second draw has been **undecided for twenty-five days**. The inherited readings still travel as day numbers and not as names, and **a day number does not move at a month turn and a name does.**
- **The next phase writes Chapters 129–138, Movement 4, *The Unfit Category*, and the prompt for it is `workspace/volume-03/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`, which carries the ten cards and was repaired with the batch in the same pass.** **It opens on day 211, the first of the eighth month, a Sunday**, and the batch's first job is a figure nobody in this holding can say: **the seventh month's compost line.** Movement 4 serves the inventory, sends the copies out again into four hands and down two roads, sacrifices the orchard's commercial harvest to keep the record alive, and brings rootwoken growth up through the abandoned rows, and **it is not an attack and it is not a symbol.** **The day letter is looked at on day 211 and not on day 241**, and the day letter began on the sixth of last month and runs a month and the households named the first to look at it.
- **Batch 0004 must not spend a word of the confession's consequences that this batch has not earned.** Sera Quill has gone, the statement cannot be corroborated, the sealed record stays sealed, the fragment is in one man's hand and a drawer and a person nobody can name, **the name Brinewake is out of Volume 03 entirely and is Volume 04's to recover from her own mouth**, and **the question is *whose order is it*, which nobody in this fen can answer and which the batch may not turn into an answer by anybody's mouth.**

## State after Volume 03 Batch 0004 (Chapters 129–138, days 211–230) — MOVEMENT 4

**Movement 4 served the inventory, put a trapdoor on the page, sent the copies out again into four hands and down two roads, priced a hundred and forty-one trees in a yard in two halves and could not finish the sum, watched four households let the rows go on their own ground, used a gap in a four-line rule and paid for it in sixteen households' water, and had a class of record declared against two of its own records without being told which two. Then a green came up out of the collars of the abandoned rows eight days later, and the volume's question was asked out loud in a yard by a licensed man and was not answered. Nothing below is closed.**

### The engine, and the four walls

1. **The class of record of an experimental character, held unfit for evidentiary use.** Four lines, correct, the word *experimental* undefined by the office, and a holder's statement that a record is experimental is itself a classification entered against the record. **The class is entered against the record and not against the holder, and therefore does not reach a person's coat.** No officer lies, obstructs or is corrupt; the officer who was not satisfied said so and said it was his right.
2. **Four walls in three weeks, every one correct, and not one of them built by this body:** the part three condition; the shape the schedule has no part for; the sealed record nobody can produce; and the class. The man of about seventy put the finding on it and it is the volume's: **every one of the four came from the other side of that gate. A wall we built ourselves we can take down. Those four we cannot see the top of and nobody is going to tell us.**
3. **A door and a trapdoor are the same door.** The only way to keep a record out of the class is to declare it experimental, and declaring it is a statement, and a statement is a classification. Nobody in nine miles has to argue with this fen to take its records out of use.
4. **The fen does not know which two of its own records are in the class, and the reader of this body entered two names in advance, in a yard, as his opinion and not a finding.** A later batch may not discover the answer from this body's own book.

### The cost, stated in fruit and in money, by the people it belongs to

- **Forty-nine pounds and one shilling** is a figure this holding has: one hundred and nine hundredweight at nine shillings, weighed at a barn door, with about twenty-two hundredweight set aside for four houses. **The price is not in this body's book, was asked for on paper, and was not sent.** The sum was done in two halves and the halves were not added, not averaged and not presented as one figure. **The honest total is forty-nine pounds and one shilling and *not held*.**
- **The first sum in eleven years about something this body does not own, and the worst of it is not the number:** this holding has published every figure it has for eleven years and has never once published a figure about what a person four hundred miles off is losing, because it was not its money.
- **Four households let the rows go on the tenth, in daylight, on their own ground, on the strength of no advice and after this body had asked them one question that had not yet arrived.** A hundred and forty-one trees of bearing left standing. The buyer is gone and will not come back and the next season's fruit has no market anybody knows of. **The word for what they did was available in that yard all day and was used by nobody in it, and the fen declined to use it, and the fen entered that a body four hundred miles off does not get to name a thing four houses did with their own ground.**
- **The seedwright's objection stands in the book and is not answered:** *a row of trees that nobody works is not a record and it is not a sacrifice until something happens in it, and if nothing happens in it then four households have given up a year of fruit for nothing and this fen put the idea in their heads.* The engineer's answer stands beside it and is not a defense: *I did not. They had a buyer who is gone and a paper they cannot be got at. I wrote nine lines they did not ask for and nine days too late. If this was my doing it was done before I knew their fruit existed.*
- **Ninety-six pounds against eighty pounds eight**, on a head that is down four inches and has not come back, with the number of dry weeks in a year entered as not known and no year put on the figure. **One dry week costs the sixteen households more than a month of the compost line costs this holding.**
- **The fourth line of the households' sheet has been read out twice and not answered once**, and the fen entered that it did not know what four houses had done until four days after they had done it, and that the not-asking is correct, and that a body which sets out to find a man has to be ready for the answer.
- **The four lines came down the four-day road on a rider the occupier hired at her own cost on the tenth, four days one way, and this body did not arrange that, did not pay for it, and has not been told what it cost.** The man this fen hired on the ninth with its question could not have brought them down before the seventeenth. **Thirty-two shillings of hired road were beaten home by a household's own expense, and the fen entered that it did not ask the price because it was afraid of the figure.** A later batch may not be credited with the sheet, may not say what that rider cost, and may not have the four lines arrive by any cheaper road.

### Seven recorded deviations from the batch's own cards, and the review pass that forced three of them

**An independent review returned twenty-one findings and all twenty-one are closed. No scene was replaced, no card was dropped, and the planned plot of all ten chapters is unchanged. The full list is in `state/continuity.md` under *The Batch 0004 review pass*.**

**The three the review forced are all the road, and they now stand as one rule on the face of three chapters and of the continuity file: the plateau road is four days up and four days down, a man is four shillings a day, a whole hire is thirty-two shillings entered four on the day he is hired and twenty-eight when he is paid off, and a man hired on day X and out on day X is back in the fen on day X plus eight.**

1. **The man who comes down the road in Ch 138 was hired on the twelfth, not on the eighteenth and not on the sixteenth.** The card had him in the fen two days after he was hired, and the draft's own repair to the sixteenth was also impossible, because a man cannot be hired on the day a licensed man writes the page that goes into his satchel. **He is at a windlass on the sixteenth and in this fen at the seventh hour of the evening of the twentieth.**
2. **The growth came up on the sixteenth and not on the eighteenth, and the figure on the households' own sheet is six days and not eight**, because a sheet written on a shelf four hundred miles off on the eighteenth cannot be in this yard on the twentieth. **The drafted line *found out four days afterwards* is now correct rather than wrong, and the whole of the batch's *eight days* has become *six days*.**
3. **The four lines came down on a rider the occupier hired at her own cost, not on the man this fen hired**, because the fen's man was on the road from the ninth to the seventeenth and the sheet is read out here on the fourteenth. **This body spent thirty-two shillings on a road and was beaten home by a household's own expense, which is the batch's own argument and not a patch.**
4. **The leak is measured on the morning of the thirteenth, not on the afternoon of the twelfth.**
5. **Ch 134's objection and answer fall on the eleventh and are made against this body's own sheet, because four days of road means the fen did not know what four houses had done until the fourteenth, and the chapter now says the seventeenth in three places where it said the fourteenth.**
6. **The man at the second house does not stand at the shelf well on the first, because lock 11 forbids him on the shelf in any narrated scene of this batch, and the ambiguity is killed on the page.**
7. **A shortfall of three feet nine inches does not decompose into three and a half feet and half an inch; that is inherited and the batch's own figure, three feet ten inches, does.**

All seven are in `state/continuity.md` under *VOLUME 03 — BATCH 0004*.

### Phase handoff, Volume 03 Batch 0004 → Batch 0005

- Chapters 129–138 are in `workspace/volume-03/batch-0004/` and summarized one compact entry per chapter in `state/chapter-summaries.md`. The volume's outline is `outline/volume-03.md`; the batch's governing prompt is `workspace/volume-03/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`.

- **Length: 29,333 words across ten chapters by `wc -w` over the chapter files including their chapter headings, and 2,781–3,120 per chapter, re-measured after the review pass.** Inside the 27,000–30,000 band and inside 2,550–3,120 at every chapter. **Ch 136 and Ch 138 are at or within a word of 3,120 and a later writer must not lengthen either.**
- **Staging.** Chs 129–133, 135, 136 and 137 are at Lantern Reach. The shelf is narrated from outside in documents and paid messengers only, and **no character is on the shelf and back at the fen inside ten days.** Three hired men went up the four-day road in this batch, hired on the fourth, the ninth and the twelfth, and **no batch may make the road shorter and no batch may make a man cheaper than four shillings a day or a whole hire less than thirty-two shillings, and a man hired on day X and out on day X is back in the fen on day X plus eight.**
- **Five dates and figures are settled by this batch and are now fixed:** the eighth month's compost line **falls on the first of next month, day 241, a Tuesday, and every cost of this month is charged against it**; the well at Cauldron Reach is **five feet and two inches on the sixteenth of this month**; the count of the seventh column is **twenty-seven on the sixteenth**, and was twenty-six on the fourteenth when this body produced it at the office at the foot of the plateau, and the fen does not read a door on a day it has not appointed; the boards are **day minus sixty-six and one hundred and sixty-four on the twentieth**; the tin is **day minus nineteen and two hundred and eleven on the twentieth**; and **the draw was attempted once, on the twelfth, and paid for on the twelfth and is not recovered in this volume.**
- **The next phase writes Chapters 139–147, the volume's climax and resolution, *Copies And A Migration*.** It opens on **day 231, the twenty-first of the eighth month** and it **owes the eighth month's compost line on day 241, the first of the ninth, a Tuesday**, on which every cost of Batch 0004's month and Batch 0005's first twenty days comes against one line. **The living archive seed comes out of the walled yard before the declaration can reach it; the evidence is published in copied ledgers and local memory; the declaration that the records are unsafe is made and a migration of farmers begins, which is not a flight; and the question the volume leaves standing is whose order the eleven cuts were given in, which was asked out loud in a yard four hundred miles off and was not answered.**
- **Batch 0005 must not spend an answer the batch has not earned.** The fen does not know which two of its records are in the class and may not find out from its own book. The growth in the rows is not read and not interpreted and nobody may say the telling is still going on and nobody may say it has stopped. The rows are the four households' decision and may not be called a sacrifice. The price is not in the fen's book. The statement cannot be corroborated. **The name Brinewake is out of Volume 03 entirely and is Volume 04's to recover from her own mouth.** **Sera Quill is not on the fen and no chapter may have a letter from her and no chapter may resolve what she gave.**

- **Volume 03 Batch 0005 has now been reviewed and repaired in place.** Twelve findings from `logs/batch-0005.review.log` plus one found while applying them; the record is `reviews/volume-03-batch-0005.findings.md` and the account of what changed is in `state/continuity.md` under *The Batch 0005 review pass, and what it changed*. **Nothing in this section is reopened by the pass and nothing in it is closed by it.** The figures the pass corrected had been certified as verified and are now right: **the improvement on the eighth month's line is one pound nineteen shillings and sevenpence, not one pound three shillings and fifteen pence**, and **the six days of the keep are one shilling and sevenpence, a total and not a day's rate**, so there is no ten shillings and sixpence anywhere in the volume. **The count of copies this body is able to check is one and four, not two and three**, because the well house door is one of the five places and is not a copy of anything; the two and three was said in a room in Ch 145 and taken back in the same room, and the fen has entered that it stood a day against its own figure. **The man of about seventy's tenth bring-out is the evening of the twenty-seventh of the eighth month, and Ch 142 sits entirely on that day.** **The close should not spend this pass again.**

## State after Volume 03 Batch 0005 (Chapters 139–147, days 231–250) — MOVEMENT 5, AND THE VOLUME CLOSE

**Movement 5 closed the eighth month on its own figures, wrote down four blanks before it knew what was in them, put a declaration four lines long on the page that says the truth of what this holding says is not in question, put the seed-route ledger in five places, and watched four carts and a hired waggon come up a fen road it did not lay, and it ends on a question nobody is going to answer, in a book with no charter, and a man of seventy-one told it to write it again next year. NOTHING IN THE MOVEMENT IS CLOSED, AND NOTHING IN THE MOVEMENT WAS CLOSED BY THE VOLUME AUDIT: the close repaired figures, dates and quotation marks, settled the beat's day on the page, and closed the volume. Every thread below is live and every one of them is carried whole. VOLUME 03 IS AUDITED AND CLOSED; the close-up is `reviews/volume-03.findings.md` and the audit record is `state/continuity.md` under *VOLUME 03 — THE CLOSE*.**

### The engine of the volume, and it is a form and not a man

1. **The class of record of an experimental character, declared twice.** Against **two records** on the sixteenth of the eighth month, neither named, and against **this holder's records** on the twenty-seventh, four lines, correct, with the sentence *nothing in this line states that anything the holder has said is untrue*. **The fen still does not know which two records are in the class, has not been told, did not ask, and Volume 04 may not have anybody tell it.**
2. **The fourth wall became five, and the fifth is a reading.** The part three condition; the shape the schedule has no part for; the sealed record nobody can produce; the class; and now **the thing this body is looking after and cannot reach, which is a walled yard at the first house on a shelf four hundred miles off.** Not one of the five was built by this body and not one of the five can be taken down.
3. **A declared record can still be published, and that is the first instrument in the manuscript that separates the standing of a page from the standing of a person.** Being unsafe is a fact about an office and not about a page. A copy in five places can be listed by an office and cannot be unwritten by one, and the man of about fifty said that in a yard and then said that he does not know whether it is a victory and will not call it one in a yard.
4. **The two sentences the volume ends on, and they have both to be sayable:** **a thing which survives in more places than one office can hold is not safe, and it is not lost.** The fen entered that it does not know which of them the reader of this body believes, that it never will, and that it has not asked him.

### The cost, in fruit, in money, in pails, and in people this body cannot count

- **The eighth month's compost line: specification four hundred hundredweight, three journeys at twenty-five hundredweight each, shortfall four hundred and seventy-five hundredweight, ninety-five pounds, the run eight months not discharged in eight.** Ninety-five is worse than the seventh month's eighty pounds eight by fourteen pounds and twelve shillings.
- **The first movement in eight months in this body's favour is one pound, nineteen shillings and sevenpence**, a penny off the price of a hundredweight at Cray, **and nobody has told this body why and the fen has not written and asked, which is the first time in eleven years it has not written and asked about a price.**
- **A yard in Fennmere has four hundred and seventy-five hundredweight at three shillings and ninepence, not tested, and the yard behind the shed is being drained.** The difference between that and Cray's new price is three pounds, nineteen shillings and twopence, the tin has four pounds and fivepence, and one shilling and threepence would be left. **The person who keeps the money said the difference is not the point and that the point is that in three months this holding would have no yard, no money and no line.**
- **The low quarter: four wells off, forty-one houses, four hundred and forty-one people against three hundred five months back, nine hundred and ten pails bought in at a shilling, forty-five pounds ten shillings.** Entered from **a miller's letter and not from the man**, and this holding cannot check one word of it and did not write to ask.
- **The four households' price for the season is still not in this body's book and may not be produced.** The honest total is forty-nine pounds and one shilling and *not held*, and it was not complete at the end of the volume and was not completed.

- **Staging.** Eight of the nine chapters are at Lantern Reach or on the fen road. **Ch 139 and Ch 141 are narrated from outside on a shelf four hundred miles off with no fen character in them. Ch 140 is narrated from outside at Cray, nine hundred miles, in a room with about nine people in it, and Tova Reed is in it as the trust's steward and not as a member of this holding.** No character is on the shelf and back at the fen inside ten days. **The only hired man in the batch is the one hired on the fourth of the eighth month whose pay-off day was never entered, and the plateau road is four days up and four days down and no batch may make it shorter and a man may not be cheaper than four shillings a day or a whole hire less than thirty-two shillings, and a man hired on day X and out on day X is back in the fen on day X plus eight.**
- **Four figures are settled by this batch and are now fixed, not open, and the next writer may not re-derive them:** the eighth month's line is **four hundred and seventy-five hundredweight and ninety-five pounds with a run of eight months not discharged in eight**; the price movement at Cray is **a penny on four hundred and seventy-five hundredweight, which is four hundred and seventy-five pence, which is one pound, nineteen shillings and sevenpence**; the low quarter holds **forty-one houses and four hundred and forty-one people against three hundred five months back**; and **the count of the readings of the seventh column is twenty-eight on the twenty-first of the eighth, twenty-nine on the thirtieth and thirty on the tenth of the ninth.** The boards are **day minus sixty-six** and the tin is **day minus nineteen**, and both rules were **derived out loud in a yard in Ch 143** because two figures in the batch's own card did not come out of them.
- **The next phase is the Volume 03 close, not prose.** The prompt is `workspace/volume-03/volume-close/PROMPT.md`. **No chapters beyond 147.**
- **Volume 04 is *Salt Without Rain*, Chapters 148–196**, and its card is in `outline/series.md`: migration from the dry Salt March tests whether the repaired network can feed people without exhausting a new region; the cooperative has become a symbol but its own wells are still fragile; **a restored well produces an intelligent rootwoken oasis that refuses extraction**; the team must distinguish a territorial living community from a monster nest; **the Pale Orchard enters and Soren Rill becomes an adversarial guide**; and **Marek hides a rootwoken migration prediction and Tova refuses to anchor him afterward.** **The name Brinewake is owed to that volume and has to come out of Sera Quill's own mouth.**

## Phase handoff, Volume 03 close → Volume 04, *Salt Without Rain*, Chapters 148–196

**VOLUME 03 IS CLOSED. VOLUME 04 IS NOT WRITTEN, NOT PLANNED, AND NOT PROMPTED. This handoff is the whole of what carries across, and it is a handoff and not an outline.** The next phase is a Volume 04 batch plan or its Batch 0001, whichever the phase system selects, and the prompt for it does not exist yet and this phase did not write one.

### The four things Volume 04 inherits that are not negotiable

1. **The next ordinary reading of the well at Cauldron Reach is the fourth hour on the sixteenth of the ninth month, day 256, by the occupier, by her own nail, in her own yard, in her own hand, said out loud before it is written down. It is the first clock Volume 04 owns and no batch may skip it and no batch may move it.** The whole of Volume 03's eighth month and nine days of the ninth went by without one, and that is a fact about a practice and not a failure.
2. **The name Brinewake is owed to Volume 04 and has to come out of Sera Quill's own mouth, uncorroborated and uncheckable by anybody including her. It may not come from an office, a log, a district index, a form, a clerk or a licensed man, and nobody may guess at it, and nobody may say that the name exists.** The name is nowhere in Volume 03 — not in narration, not in a document, not in a form, not in a guess.
3. **No chapter of Volume 04 may answer whose order it is.** The question was asked once in a yard by a licensed man and was not answered, the count of asking is two and neither is answered, the count of times it has been available to a room and not taken is three, and the fen has written down that it is not going to find out and is going to keep asking. **A chapter that answers it spends the series' central question on a chapter.**
4. **A Volume 04 draw, if there is one, is a third use and not a second, must be paid for in the same chapter in somebody else's water, and must not be recovered.** The standing rule is four lines, in force, used once on the twelfth of the eighth month, and it says nothing whatever about what comes after the stop.

### The live Volume 03 threads, whole, none of them closed by the audit

**The two tables above are the threads and they stand, and the volume audit changed no thread in either of them.** The tightest of them for a Volume 04 writer: **the class of record, two of this holder's records in it, neither named, and nobody in Volume 04 may tell this body which two; the sealed compensation record, producible only to the person named in it on that person's own request, which Volume 04 has a restitution beat to run against, so the request has to be made by a person and not by a body; the migration, four carts and a hired waggon, three destinations named and one not, the waggon went on in the afternoon and this body does not know where and did not ask, and two hundred and eighty households on that road have said out loud that they are staying, which is not loyalty and is not evidence that this holding is worth anything; the low quarter, four wells off, forty-one houses, four hundred and forty-one people, nine hundred and ten pails at a shilling, forty-five pounds ten, out of a miller's letter and not from the man, which this holding cannot check one word of; the ninth month's compost line, which falls on the first of the tenth, day 271, and which this holding will not know until the morning of that day, now on a run of eight months not discharged in eight; the seed-route ledger's five places against the copies' four, a new count entered on one page with the other, and the two are not to be added; the day letter, which came off the stone at the fourth house on the eighth of the ninth and did not go back on, and the fen has not asked whether it runs again and has not been told; the green cut off the collars on the twenty-fifth of the eighth with the cuttings left on the ground, not read, not interpreted, not an attack and not a symbol; the Nia Vale letter, sealed in a shut drawer and unopened across about a hundred and ninety days of season, which no chapter may open, quote or make a scene; the fourth line of the standing list, what this body is looking after and cannot reach, which may not be reduced to the third line, which is what this body does not know; and the man at the second house, who is not in this fen and not on the shelf, whose coming back is not decided and may not be invented or hinted at.**

### What Volume 04 may not inherit as confusion

**The volume's own day anchor, because the close wrote it wrong once and it is the rule a Volume 04 planner would build a naming table from: months run thirty days, day 151 is the first of the SIXTH month and a Wednesday, and day 250 is the tenth of the ninth and a Thursday. The close first wrote the fifth month. It is the sixth — the Batch 0002 table has 151–180 the sixth, `state/current.md` has the volume running from the first of the sixth, and Ch 99's own first line puts the first of this month on a Wednesday. The ten weekday checks are keyed to day numbers, so nothing in Volume 03 moved, but a table built from the wrong anchor would mis-date every month of the new volume by thirty days.**

**Two naming conventions for past months are in force in the manuscript, one in Batches 0001 and 0002 and one in Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005, and a Volume 04 writer takes the table from the batch prompt and does not derive one and does not assume either.** The boards are *day minus sixty-six* and the tin is *day minus nineteen*, and both rules are derived out loud in Chapter 143. The compost chain is four entries and not eight, and the eight are the run. **Day 238, the twenty-eighth of the eighth month, is narrated nowhere in Volume 03, and Volume 04 opens on day 251.** The well's shortfall inherits as **three and a half feet and four inches** out of three feet ten inches, because Chapter 137 is the only chapter whose decomposition closes and four earlier chapters' half-inch figures are recorded and deliberately not repaired; **a Volume 04 chapter may not re-derive the total and may not make the half inch do arithmetic, and the same rule that protected Ch 137 protects Volume 04.** The plateau road is four days up and four days down, a man is four shillings a day, a whole hire is thirty-two shillings entered four on the day he is hired and twenty-eight when he is paid off, and a man hired on day X and out on day X is back in the fen on day X plus eight. **The beat's day is settled at 171 and may not be restated; a document that must name it says *the twenty-first of a month three months back* from a scene inside days 211 to 240 and *the twenty-first of a month four months back* from a scene inside days 241 to 270.** The tin is written **four pounds five** in eleven places and **four pounds and fivepence** in the two where the difference is done, and Volume 04 writes it in full the first time.

**The well's own readings, by day, because a Volume 04 chapter that names one of them has to name it in the frame its own batch table gives and the two conventions above will produce two different names for the same day.** Day 136 read nine feet and is *the sixteenth of a month two months back* from a seventh-month scene and *the sixteenth of a month three months back* from a seventh-month scene in Batch 0003's table; day 164 five feet and a half; **day 166 five feet five inches**; day 170 five feet four inches; day 180 five feet two inches; day 196 five feet three inches; day 226 five feet two inches; and day 256 is the next ordinary reading and is owed. **The shortfall of three feet ten inches closes against day 136 and only against day 136, because nine feet less five feet two inches is three feet ten inches, and no Volume 04 chapter may measure it against anything else.** Ch 118's *the sixteenth of a month two months back* is therefore right and was re-argued and left alone at the review of the close.

**And one mechanical rule, so that no later pass repeats the close's mistake: the closing paragraph of a speech carried over paragraphs carries the close and does not open, so a chapter built on one can hold an odd number of quotation marks. Chs 123, 126, 127 and 128 do, and that is correct. No paragraph may end with its speech open; that is the property to audit, and an even count of marks is not a test of anything in this manuscript.**


### Three things the Volume 04 card may not be read as, on the strength of the volume that has just closed

**A restored well is a class-of-record question as well as a water question, because this holding has spent a whole volume learning that the standing of a page and the standing of a person are two different things and that a thing surviving in five places is not safe and is not lost.** **A rootwoken oasis that refuses extraction must not be a monster, because the volume's deepest finding is that soundness is not a hand, and this manuscript does not have a mechanism that is a villain: rung 5 of the antagonist ladder was inside the protagonist's own history and it was correct in every document it issued.** **An adversarial guide is the Volume 03 shape of a records officer who is right in every document, so Soren Rill's positions have to be defensible on the page and not merely inconvenient, and he has to be someone whose refusals this body would have found plausible.**

## State after Volume 04 Batch 0001 (Chapters 148–157, days 251–268) — VOLUME 04 IS OPEN

**The ninth month opened on a bill this holding cannot pay and closed on four hours it has given away. A licensed supply has been put into a yard in four correct lines by an office that has not lied once. A rule of four lines could not be satisfied and was broken at twenty past four in the morning, and a house in a quarter nine miles off went off the next morning and has not come back. A man who reads ground said no to a word and then asked for a pail, a measure and an hour, and this holding gave him all three with no promise and a time. And an engineer of record has said a thing in one room that is not in this holding's book and has entered the count of it and not the thing. Nothing below is closed.**

### The seven live questions Volume 04 has, and which one each batch owns

1. **Whose order is it** — Volume 03's question, asked twice, answered never, **and no chapter of Volume 04 may answer it.** The count of times it has been asked is two and the count of times it has been available to a room and not taken is three, and both stand.
2. **Is the telling still going on** — struck out of this body's own mouth in front of witnesses a month two months back, and **nobody may say it is and nobody may say it has stopped**, and the green in the older row is unread and unnamed.
3. **Are they walking toward a thing that is walking with them** — **the volume's own question, asked once, in a yard of about thirty, and not taken.** The count is one and one, and the two counts are not to be added to the count of the first question, and the difference between the third and the first is on the page: an order has a person at the end of it and this body has not got one.
4. **What is under the ground in the low quarter, and is it a community or a thing** — **Movement 3, and the word for it is refused and is not on a page.** Nobody in this book may supply it.
5. **Whose order the eleven of next month is, and whether the engineer of record told one person or a body** — **Volume 04's relationship fault line, and it does not resolve in this volume.** The apology is owed in Movement 4 and the refusal to anchor is owed in Movement 3.
6. **Whether a body that has become a name can fill in a form about four hundred and forty-one people it cannot see** — **the volume's political question and the Crown's frame, and it runs to the end of the volume.** The count of noes is six and the count of questions asked in a yard and not got an answer is two.
7. **What was the fourth cart doing and where did it go** — **carried from Volume 03 and not resolved here, and the not asking is a month old.**

### Volume 04 plan locks

- **Volume 04 is *Salt Without Rain*, Chapters 148–196, days 251–350, and the outline was written by the phase that drafted Batch 0001.** Read `outline/volume-04.md` for the eleven guardrails, the five movements and the batch map. **The card in `outline/series.md` is the volume's contract and the outline is its execution, and where the two differ the series card is the contract.**
- **The volume's first clock was day 256 and it is paid, read, and not decomposed. The next is day 286 and Movement 3 owns it.**
- **The stage at the end of Batch 0001 is Stage 3, Drawwright, with three draws spent in three volumes and none recovered. Stage 4 arrives in Movement 3 by use, and it arrives as absorbing a failure sensation and becoming a maintenance bottleneck, not as a larger output. No draw, no anchor, no human living anchor, no seedheart, no caretaker link, no deep archive and no memory read in Movement 2.**
- **The fourth cart, the man at the second house, the Nia Vale letter, the sealed record's request, the pressure pulse and the third exchange requirement are all inherited and all untouched, and none of them is Batch 0002's work.**

### Unreconciled between the series card and the volume outline, flagged by the Batch 0001 review and resolved by nobody

- **The Volume 04 card in `outline/series.md` gives the volume a next question that `outline/volume-04.md` does not carry.** The card's *Next question* reads: *What is happening beneath the sealed Glassward, and can a bridge carry responsibility rather than only water?* **The volume outline carries the two inherited questions from Volume 03 — whose order it is, and whether the telling is still going on — and adds the volume's own third, about people walking toward water and whether they are walking toward a thing walking with them, and it is silent on the card's third.** The line above says the series card is the contract where the two documents differ, so the silence is a real gap and not a permission.
- **What is and is not done about it.** Nothing was changed in either document and no plot moved. **No chapter of Volume 04 answers the card's question and no chapter may.** The Glassward and the sealed record stay sealed; the record is producible only on the named person's own request and no request has been made; and *a bridge carrying responsibility rather than only water* is a Stage 4 idea that this volume reaches as **a man who is the only person who can hold a pattern being entered as a bottleneck and not as a system**, which is the same thought in this volume's own vocabulary and is not the same sentence. **A later writer may make that rhyme explicit in a Movement 4 scene in which a body has to answer for ground it did not draw. Nobody may open the Glassward, produce the sealed record, or name what is beneath either.**
- **The next question belongs to Volume 05's card** and is not this phase's to rewrite. If the two are ever reconciled it will be a Volume 04 close or Volume 05 outline decision, recorded there and not silently.

NOBODY MAY REQUEST IT, NOBODY MAY BE NAMED IN IT, NOTHING IN IT MAY BE OPENED, AND NOBODY MAY SAY THAT A REQUEST EXISTS. Sera Quill did not make the request, was not asked to, and may not be asked and may not say that she is the person named** |
