| **sixty pounds ten, the Nia Vale letter, the man at the second house, the third exchange requirement, the pressure pulse, the fourth line of the standing list** | **carried exactly as Volume 03 and Batch 0001 left them** | **none of the six is Batch 0003's work. No chapter may open the letter, reduce the fourth line to the third, invent the man at the second house, describe the third condition, diagnose the pulse, or bring sixty pounds ten into any figure** |

### The seven live questions Volume 04 has, and where each one stands after Movement 2

1. **Whose order is it** — asked twice, answered never, **and no chapter of Volume 04 may answer it.** The count of the times it has been asked is two and the count of the times it has been available to a room and not taken is three, and both stand. **Nothing in Movement 2 moved either count.**
2. **Is the telling still going on** — struck out of this body's own mouth in front of witnesses a month two months back, **nobody may say it is and nobody may say it has stopped**, and the green in the older row four hundred miles off is unread, unnamed, and **three of the four looked and one did not and said he is not going to.**
3. **Are they walking toward a thing that is walking with them** — the volume's own question, asked once, in a yard of about thirty. The count is one and one, and the difference between the third and the first is on the page: **an order has a person at the end of it and this body has not got one.** Nothing in Movement 2 moved either count, and **a man of about thirty-five came back up a slope and said a thing under a floor four hundred yards from that gate is not late, and that is a fact about a man and is not a finding and is not a second count.**
4. **What is under the ground in the low quarter, and is it a community or a thing** — **Movement 3, and the word for it is refused and is not on a page and was not used in Movement 2.** Nobody in this book may supply it. **Movement 3 must get to the ground and must not get to the word.**
5. **Whose order the eleventh was, and whether the engineer of record told one person or a body** — **the date fell and nothing happened, and he said so in a yard, in four sentences, and asked that a word be entered nowhere in this book.** The relationship survives only because he said it in front of people before anybody made him. **The refusal to anchor is owed in Movement 3 and the apology in Movement 4.**
6. **Whether a body that has become a name can fill in a form about four hundred and forty-one people it cannot see** — **the volume's political question, and Movement 2 moved it rather than settling it.** The fen can now do the whole sum and still cannot write the first half of the sentence; the count of noes is six; **and there is now a party on a form nine miles off that the fen cannot check either, and the two positions are entered side by side and are going to be read in the same room one day by somebody who has not been born yet.**
7. **What was the fourth cart doing and where did it go** — **carried from Volume 03, now entered as one of the four things this body does not know, and not resolved.** The not asking is a month old and is entered as a month and not as a day.

### Volume 04 plan locks after Movement 2

- **Volume 04 is *Salt Without Rain*, Chapters 148–196, days 251–350, and the outline is `outline/volume-04.md`.** Read it for the eleven guardrails, the five movements and the batch map. **The card in `outline/series.md` is the volume's contract and the outline is its execution, and where the two differ the series card is the contract.**
- **Movement 3 is *The Thing That Refuses*, Chapters 168–177: a restored well on the dry road produces a rootwoken community that will not be drawn from, and it is not a nest and it is not a monster and it is a settlement with a food system; Stage 4 arrives here by use and arrives as absorbing a failure sensation and becoming a maintenance bottleneck, and the volume names that as a defect and not as an achievement; and the distinguishing is done by a man who has read four pieces of ground in nine years and three of them were crews with a licence.** The word *nest* is used and refused in Movement 3 and the refused word is still not on any page.
- **The stage at the end of Batch 0002 is Stage 3, Drawwright, with three draws spent in three volumes, none recovered, and the rootmark unchanged at four inches with no second mark and a third account of the arm in the book that reconciles neither of the other two. No anchor, no human living anchor, no seedheart, no caretaker link, no deep archive and no memory read in Movement 2.**
- **The fourth cart, the man at the second house, the Nia Vale letter, the sealed record's request, the pressure pulse and the third exchange requirement are all inherited and all untouched, and none of them is Batch 0003's work.**

### Still unreconciled between the series card and the volume outline, flagged by the Batch 0001 review, unresolved by Batch 0002, and flagged again here

- **The Volume 04 card in `outline/series.md` gives the volume a next question that `outline/volume-04.md` does not carry.** The card's *Next question* reads: *What is happening beneath the sealed Glassward, and can a bridge carry responsibility rather than only water?* The volume outline carries the two inherited questions from Volume 03 and adds the volume's own third, and it is silent on the card's third. The line above says the series card is the contract where the two documents differ, so the silence is a real gap and not a permission.
- **What Batch 0002 did about it, and did not do.** Nothing was changed in either document and no plot moved. **No chapter of Volume 04 answers the card's question and no chapter may.** The Glassward and the sealed record stay sealed. **A later writer may make the card's second half rhyme explicitly in Movement 3, where a man who is the only person who can hold a pattern is entered as a bottleneck and not as a system, which is the same thought in this volume's own vocabulary and is not the same sentence. Nobody may open the Glassward, produce the sealed record, or name what is beneath either.** If the two documents are ever reconciled it is a Volume 04 close or a Volume 05 outline decision, recorded there and not silently.

## State after Volume 04 Batch 0003 (Chapters 168–177, days 286–300) — MOVEMENT 3 IS DRAFTED

**The tenth month closed on a nail and a string and a round number four hundred miles off, a holder of record who put his own well back together at his own cost and was not thanked and could not be, a word used once in a yard and refused twice, a food in a row that four households have been eating since about the tenth of this month, a man who is the only person in this fen who can hold a pattern for four, a refusal in a yard of nine by the woman who wrote the four-line rule, a refusal up nine miles from below the ground, a figure that is not a number of anybody, two sums that agreed, a cart nobody said a word about, and a month that closed on a day and not a figure. Nothing below is closed.**

No chapter may open the letter, reduce the fourth line to the third, invent the man at the second house, describe the third condition, diagnose the pulse, bring sixty pounds ten into any figure, or confirm, refute, explain or give a cause to the eleventh of last month. **No chapter in Movement 4 may have the engineer of record produce a second reading** |
| **the clerk's observation** | **five spent in the chain: the door is a trapdoor, a number we cannot check, the office is not a villain, which of the two he believes, and the book cannot check her** | **none was spent in Batch 0003. ONE IS AVAILABLE IN BATCH 0004 AND MORE THAN ONE IN A BATCH WOULD BE A HABIT AND NOT A PRACTICE** |

### The seven live questions Volume 04 has, and where each one stands after Movement 3

1. **Whose order is it** — asked twice, answered never, **and no chapter of Volume 04 may answer it.** The count of the times it has been asked is two and the count of the times it has been available to a room in this holding and not taken is **six, of which three are this question**, and both stand and neither has been added to the other. **It was put into a yard of about nine on the twenty-seventh, in the same yard a man had said a thing is not to be drawn from, and nobody took it, and the fen entered that nobody asked who told them and entered the reason, which is that the question has been asked twice and answered never and that asking it a third time in front of a man who has just said no to something would be a way of not having heard him.**
2. **Is the telling still going on** — struck out of this body's own mouth in front of witnesses a month back, **nobody may say it is and nobody may say it has stopped**, and the green in the older row four hundred miles off is unread, unnamed, **and it is now entered on that shelf's own wall in a fourth line, and three of the four looked and one did not and said he is not going to.**
3. **Are they walking toward a thing that is walking with them** — the volume's own question, asked once, in a yard of about thirty. The count is one and one. **Movement 3 did not move either count and is not permitted to: what came up the road on the twenty-seventh was a refusal to draw from a thing under a quarter, and that is a thing about a ground and not a second count about a road.**
4. **What is under the ground in the low quarter, and is it a community or a thing** — **MOVEMENT 3 GOT TO THE GROUND AND DID NOT GET TO THE WORD, WHICH IS WHAT IT WAS TOLD TO DO.** It has a food system, a return, an opinion and a refusal. It is not a nest and not a monster and not a symbol and it is not redeemed, **nobody in this book may put a name on it that the man who reads it has not said, and the refused word is still not on a page.** It will not be drawn from, and no chapter of Volume 04 may have anybody draw from it.
5. **Whose order the eleventh was, and whether the engineer of record told one person or a body** — **the date fell and nothing happened and he said so in a yard and asked that a word be entered nowhere in this book. NO CHAPTER IN BATCH 0003 CONFIRMED, REFUTED, EXPLAINED OR GAVE IT A CAUSE, AND NO CHAPTER IN IT HAD HIM PRODUCE A SECOND READING.** The apology is owed in Movement 4 and has not happened.
6. **Whether a body that has become a name can fill in a form about four hundred and forty-one people it cannot see** — **Movement 3 moved it and did not settle it.** The fen can now do the whole of the licensed sum and still cannot write the first half of the sentence; it can ask a man how many there are in his own yard and be told a number that is not a number of anybody; **it can count four figures it cannot check and has entered that a figure a body cannot check is not a protection and is a debt;** and the form on the bench would stop the only supply going into that quarter and the fen has entered that it has thought about that four times in a month and has not stopped him.
7. **What was the fourth cart doing and where did it go** — **asked about once, in a yard, by a man who had not been asked to ask, and not resolved. The count of the not-known stands at four and was four a month ago, and the reader entered that he is not going to make it five and did not promise anybody that he would not.** The not asking is a month old and is entered as a month and not as a day.

### Volume 04 plan locks after Movement 3

- **Volume 04 is *Salt Without Rain*, Chapters 148–196, days 251–350, and the outline is `outline/volume-04.md`. Read it for the eleven guardrails, the five movements and the batch map. The card in `outline/series.md` is the volume's contract and the outline is its execution, and where the two differ the series card is the contract.**
- **Movement 4 is *A Basin With No Owner*, Chapters 178–187: the Crown's frame becomes an instrument, inspections with soldiers on the road, a rate, and a form that says a migration is a licensed use; the Pale Orchard does a real thing for a real reason and kills people doing it, and the volume separates the accusation from the method out loud and does not resolve either; and Tova leads the seed side away from Marek and the two of them do not work together for eleven chapters and it is not a quarrel.** The apology is owed in it. **The apology is the one thing Movement 3 explicitly set up and did not pay, and it is the first job of Batch 0004 after the compost line.**
- **The stage at the end of Batch 0003 is Stage 4, Patternkeeper, with the count of people who can hold a pattern for four in this fen at one, entered as a defect, and the rootmark unchanged at four inches with no second mark and four accounts of the arm that reconcile none of the others. No anchor, no human living anchor, no seedheart, no caretaker link, no deep archive and no memory read in Movement 3.**
- **The fourth cart, the man at the second house, the Nia Vale letter, the sealed record's request, the pressure pulse, the third exchange requirement, Brinewake and the eleventh of last month are all inherited and all untouched, and none of them is Batch 0004's work.**
- **One Fieldbook panel is available to Batch 0004. Batch 0003's went unspent and that is not a debt.**
- **One clerk's observation is available to Batch 0004.**

### Still unreconciled between the series card and the volume outline, flagged by the Batch 0001 review, unresolved by Batch 0002 and Batch 0003, and flagged again here

- **The Volume 04 card in `outline/series.md` gives the volume a next question that `outline/volume-04.md` does not carry.** The card's *Next question* reads: *What is happening beneath the sealed Glassward, and can a bridge carry responsibility rather than only water?* The volume outline carries the two inherited questions from Volume 03 and adds the volume's own third, and it is silent on the card's third. The line above says the series card is the contract where the two documents differ, so the silence is a real gap and not a permission.
- **What Batch 0003 did about it, and did not do.** Nothing was changed in either document and no plot moved. **No chapter of Volume 04 answers the card's question and no chapter may. The Glassward and the sealed record stay sealed.** **The card's second half has now been made to rhyme explicitly and in this volume's own vocabulary, in Ch 174, by a sentence the reader of this body said out loud in a yard of about nine people: a bottleneck is a man, and a system is four people and a schedule and a rule about what happens when the man is asleep, and this head has a man and a pattern and no schedule and no rule. That is a bridge carrying responsibility rather than only water, said without the word, and nobody in that yard thanked anybody for it.** If the two documents are ever reconciled it is a Volume 04 close or a Volume 05 outline decision, recorded there and not silently.

---

## State after Volume 04 Batch 0004 (Chapters 178–187, days 301–320) — MOVEMENT 4 IS DRAFTED

**The eleventh month opened on a figure that came in the same twice in a row and a word that does not agree with it, a form that says a migration along a road is a licensed use and puts this holding in a class in its own hand, soldiers on a wet road for an hour and a half and this holding's own figures read out whole to a man with a book, a seed side that went onto a road at a trust's cost and did not come back, an apology said out loud in a yard of about twenty-five in daylight with a date and an hour and a number of people in a room given in order, a party that was four words in a return and became a man who corrected this holding against himself and said a true thing and then said the method out loud after it, a dead man of thirty-one on a page and nobody in a room, the second and last use of the standing rule, perfectly satisfied, followed by the sentence this book has been building, a second man with a cart on a lane whose water nobody knows the source of, a household's eleven years of counting in a woman's hand priced at a rate it did not ask to be priced, and a man of about seventy fetched for the fifteenth time asking who counts them. Nothing below is closed.**

### THE MONTH TURN, WHICH BATCH 0005 MUST CONTINUE

**This batch crossed the month turn for the first time in Volume 04 and therefore has TWO naming tables, and the whole of the tenth month changed its name in the space of one chapter, which is the recorded reason a day number is not a name. FROM CH 187 FORWARD: THE WHOLE OF THE ELEVENTH MONTH IS *LAST MONTH*, AND EVERY ORDINAL DISTANCE ABOVE IT SHIFTS ONE BUCKET DOWN. That is, from Batch 0005's frame of days 321–350: days 1–30 *a month eleven months back*; 31–60 *a month ten months back*; 61–90 *a month nine months back*; 91–120 *a month eight months back*; 121–150 *a month seven months back*; 151–180 *a month six months back*; 181–210 *a month five months back*; 211–240 *a month four months back*; 241–270 *a month three months back*; 271–300 *a month two months back*; 301–330 *last month*; 331–350 *of this month*, *the first* … *the twentieth*. A day of the first month is a bare number only. What a chapter may never do is carry a name across the turn. The referents therefore land as follows, and the day number is the invariant and the name is the frame:**

| **sixty pounds ten, the Nia Vale letter, the man at the second house, the third exchange requirement, the pressure pulse, the eleventh of last month** | **carried exactly as Volume 03 and the three Volume 04 batches left them, and none of the six is Batch 0004's work** | **none of the six is Batch 0005's work. NO CHAPTER MAY OPEN THE LETTER, REDUCE THE FOURTH LINE OF THE STANDING LIST TO THE THIRD, INVENT THE MAN AT THE SECOND HOUSE, DESCRIBE THE THIRD CONDITION, DIAGNOSE THE PULSE, BRING SIXTY POUNDS TEN INTO ANY FIGURE, OR CONFIRM, REFUTE, EXPLAIN OR GIVE A CAUSE TO THE ELEVENTH OF A MONTH TWO MONTHS BACK. NO CHAPTER IN MOVEMENT 4 OR THE CLOSE MAY HAVE THE ENGINEER OF RECORD PRODUCE A SECOND READING** |
| **the clerk's observation** | **six spent in the chain: the door is a trapdoor, a number we cannot check, the office is not a villain, which of the two he believes, the book cannot check her, and THE FIGURES OF THIS HOLDING WERE READ OUT LOUD, IN A YARD, TO A MAN WHO CAME TO FIND OUT WHETHER THEY WERE TRUE** | **NONE IS AVAILABLE FOR BATCH 0005 ON THE COUNT IN THIS FILE, and one was available and was spent once in Batch 0004. The word *office is not a villain* spent as a speech in Ch 179 is NOT a clerk's observation and the two are different instruments** |

### The seven live questions Volume 04 has, and where each one stands after Movement 4

1. **Whose order it is** — **asked a third time, in a yard of about nine on the eighth of this month, and taken by nobody. NO CHAPTER OF VOLUME 04 MAY ANSWER IT.** The count of the times this body has asked it is three and none has been answered, and the count of the times it has been available to a room in this holding and not taken is **six, of which three are this question**, and both stand and neither has been added to the other.
2. **Is the telling still going on** — struck out of this body's own mouth in front of witnesses a month back, **nobody may say it is and nobody may say it has stopped**, and the green in the older row four hundred miles off is unread, unnamed and entered on that shelf's own wall in a fourth line. **No chapter of Batch 0004 referred to the green at all and that is the correct treatment and not an omission.**
3. **Are they walking toward a thing that is walking with them** — the volume's own question, asked once, in a yard of about thirty. The count is one and one and **MOVEMENT 4 DID NOT MOVE EITHER COUNT AND WAS NOT PERMITTED TO.** What came up the road in Movement 4 was a licensed use, a letter and a man, and a man and a method and a document, and **none of those is a second count about a road.**
4. **What is under the ground in the low quarter, and is it a community or a thing** — Movement 3 got to the ground and did not get to the word. It has a food system, a return, an opinion and a refusal. It is not a nest and not a monster and not a symbol and it is not redeemed, and **no chapter of Movement 4 went near it, and the volume's climax must decide whether it goes near it and on what ground.**
5. **Whose order the eleventh was, and whether the engineer of record told one person or a body** — **THE APOLOGY WAS PAID IN CHAPTER 182 AND IT FORGAVE NOTHING AND IT WAS NOT A RECONCILIATION.** The date fell and nothing happened and he said so in a yard and asked that a word be entered nowhere in this book. **NO CHAPTER IN BATCH 0004 CONFIRMED, REFUTED, EXPLAINED OR GAVE IT A CAUSE, AND NO CHAPTER IN IT HAD HIM PRODUCE A SECOND READING. Ch 182 is about that concealment and about nothing else.**
6. **Whether a body that has become a name can fill in a form about four hundred and forty-one people it cannot see** — **MOVEMENT 4 ANSWERED PART OF IT AND NOT THE REST.** This holding has now been **asked in writing, in a correct document, to state in its own book and on a date the number of people in the low quarter, and it has said out loud in a yard that it cannot refuse the request and cannot accept it, because refusing a question in the open is a way of saying the question and accepting it would be a way of inventing. The fen entered that the difference between those two is the whole argument of this volume in one line. The party that made the demand is correct and the method is indefensible and neither was resolved.**
7. **What was the fourth cart doing and where did it go** — **NOT ASKED ABOUT IN BATCH 0004 AND NOT RESOLVED.** The not asking is a month and a half old and is entered as a month and a half and not as a day. **The same non-asking entered twice in this batch on the same ground: the man of the north row with the cough did not ask where the second cart-man's water comes from, and the reader of this body did not ask about a gate count the officer had told them was not a record. Both non-askings are entered with the reason and both reasons are the same sentence: it is not my row, and a man who writes things down is not a man who goes and stands in gates asking people what they have stopped doing.**

### Volume 04 plan locks after Movement 4

- **Volume 04 is *Salt Without Rain*, Chapters 148–196, and the outline is `outline/volume-04.md`. Read it for the eleven guardrails, the five movements and the batch map. The card in `outline/series.md` is the volume's contract and the outline is its execution, and where the two differ the series card is the contract.**
- **Movement 4 IS DRAFTED. Batch 0005 is Chapters 188–196: THE LONG PAN, and it is the volume's climax at 186–192 and the volume's resolution at 192–196. ITS PROMPT DOES NOT YET EXIST — `workspace/volume-04/batch-0005/` is not on disk and never has been, and this line previously certified a card that was not there. It must be written before that phase drafts, and it must be the only next phase created.** The batch map in `outline/volume-04.md` allocates 188–196 to the climax and the resolution, and Chapter 187 is where this batch's own work ends, so Batch 0005 begins on day 321 and opens on a day that is not a reading day and not the first of a month, and its first job is the first of the twelfth month.**
- **The stage at the end of Batch 0004 is Stage 4, Patternkeeper, with the count of people who can hold a pattern for four in this fen at one, entered as a defect, and the rootmark unchanged at four inches with no second mark and four accounts of the arm that reconcile none of the others, and the draw of the thirteenth not entered as a fifth. No anchor, no human living anchor, no seedheart, no caretaker link, no deep archive and no memory read in Movement 4.**
- **The fourth cart, the man at the second house, the Nia Vale letter, the sealed record's request, the pressure pulse, the third exchange requirement, Brinewake and the eleventh of a month two months back are all inherited and all untouched, and none of them is Batch 0005's work.**

### Still unreconciled between the series card and the volume outline, flagged by the Batch 0001 review, unresolved by Batches 0002, 0003 and 0004, and flagged again here

The line above says the series card is the contract where the two documents differ, so the silence is a real gap and not a permission.

The Glassward and the sealed record stay sealed.** **The card's second half has now been made to rhyme twice in this volume's own vocabulary, and the second rhyme is the batch's own subject rather than the reader's: in Ch 174 a man who is the only person who can hold a pattern was entered as a bottleneck and not a system, and in Ch 185 the rule this body wrote about a stop was entered as a rule about waiting that has been called a rule about care, and a body that obeys it perfectly for ever and never does a thing again will look exactly like a body that is being careful. A bridge that carries responsibility and not only water is the sentence the volume is ending on, and it is on a page, in a yard, in the plainest words available, and it is not a speech about a bridge.** If the two documents are ever reconciled it is a Volume 04 close or a Volume 05 outline decision, recorded there and not silently.

## Raised by the Batch 0004 review pass, and left open deliberately

**These four are not Batch 0005's plot and Batch 0005 must not try to absorb them. Three are craft debts owed to the manuscript at large and one is a mechanical fault in a file this batch did not write. They are recorded here so that the next writer inherits them as known quantities instead of rediscovering them.**

### 1. The meta-language sweep — the largest single craft debt in the manuscript, and a controller-level decision, not a writer's quiet edit

**The Batch 0004 review found eight instances of narrator-facing vocabulary in that batch's ten chapters. All eight are fixed, and Volume 04 Batch 0004 is now clean. The finding underneath them is not fixed and is not Batch 0004's to fix.**

The reviewer's regression check, which is the whole reason this is flagged rather than patched:

| | Volume 01 | Volume 02 | Volume 03 | Volume 04 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| *this batch* | 28 | 72 | 127 | 98 |
| *this chapter* | — | 3 | 24 | 5 |

Plus *the chapter says*, scattered across all four. **Three completed volume closes and their review passes did not catch it. Nothing about it is local to any one batch, and a fix confined to one batch leaves the manuscript in exactly the state it is in now: correct in Volume 04 Batch 0004 and wrong in the other 177 chapters.**

**Why it must not be swept silently, and what the sweep has to decide first.** A sweep of this size rewrites prose in every volume and invalidates every recorded word count in this file and in `state/continuity.md` and `state/current.md`. It also has a genuine judgement call in it that the reviewer identified and that must be settled on the record, not by pattern:

- ***This book* is diegetic and is not a target.** It is the holding's own record book; the fen enters into it; thirty-five uses in Batch 0004 alone are correct. **Leave it.**
- ***Batch* and *chapter* have no diegetic referent and cannot be given one.** These are the targets.
- ***This volume* is genuinely arguable and nine uses in Batch 0004 were left standing on purpose**, because a bound record book has volumes and *the second in this volume* can be read as the fen counting inside its own record. **The sweep must rule on this class explicitly.** It is the one place where a well-meaning sweep could damage the book by removing a word that means something.

**The replacement vocabulary that Batch 0004 established and that a sweep should adopt for consistency**, so the manuscript does not end up with four different answers: *this holding*, *the book*, *this holding's book*, *the day in hand*, *of the month*. Every one of the eight fixes is listed in `state/continuity.md` under *Fixes applied to the drafted Batch 0004 on review*.

### 2. A format limit for the `Entered ... by the clerk` block, owed to Batch 0005 and onward

**Batch 0004 carries thirty-five of these blocks, and four chapters carry five apiece. The review was right that this is the batch's weakest prose and right that the recorded mechanical gate does not catch it, because the gate audits paragraph closure, non-ASCII, month naming and locks and audits nothing structural.**

**The block is not the problem and must not be cut down in quantity or flattened in form.** It is the instrument this volume is about and the book's whole formal argument, and the review found the gate passing. The problem is only that a documented format ran five deep in four chapters without a rule saying it should not.

**So the rule, for Batch 0005 onward: no more than three `Entered ... by the clerk` blocks in any one chapter, and the chapter carrying the volume's climax is not to be one of them.** Batch 0004 is left as drafted. This is recorded as a forward limit and not applied retroactively.

### 3. `workspace/volume-04/batch-0001/chapter-0156.md` does not end in a newline

Found while recomputing word counts for the repair pass, and **not fixed, because it belongs to a batch this phase did not write.** The sibling fault in `chapter-0178.md` was fixed, because that file is this batch's.

**It is not cosmetic.** A `cat`-based word count merges the last word of one file into the first word of the next at every missing-newline join, so this one file is why the manuscript figure recorded before the repair pass, 565,220, was **two words low** — the true per-file sum was 565,222. **Any future count taken with `cat` across a directory containing this file will be short by one, and any count taken per-file will disagree with it.** One byte fixes it.

### 4. The Batch 0005 prompt does not exist — BLOCKING, and already corrected in the plan locks above

Recorded in the plan locks at the head of this section and in full at the end of `state/continuity.md`. **`workspace/volume-04/batch-0005/` is not on disk and never has been, and this file previously certified a card at that path that was not there.** Everything Batch 0005 inherits is correct and is listed in those two places; what is missing is the prompt itself, and it must be written before that phase drafts and must be the only next phase created.

# State after Volume 04 Batch 0005 — the volume's last batch, and the volume's draft is COMPLETE

**Chapters 188–196, days 321–350, are drafted. Volume 04 is now 196 chapters and 143,211 words and the whole of it exists on disk. NOTHING FURTHER IS DRAFTED UNTIL THE CLOSE PHASE RUNS.** The next phase is **Volume 04 close**, its prompt is owed at `workspace/volume-04/volume-close/PROMPT.md`, and **the Volume 05 prompt does not exist and this batch did not write one and must not.**

## The two things this batch found and fixed, and neither may be re-broken

1. **The weekday was wrong, and the wrongness is now a fact in the book.** Day 331 is a **Monday**, not a Saturday; day 330 is a **Sunday**, not a Friday. `state/current.md`, Ch 178 and Ch 187 all had it two days out. **Ch 178 and Ch 187 are closed prose and were not edited.** The error is on the page instead, as an entry by Gael Senn in a yard before the fourth hour on day 321, both figures entered, neither improved on, and the count of the two days this book has got wrong in its own hand is two and does not come down.
2. **The reading day was wrong, and a reading had been silently skipped.** The standing rule is the fourth hour on the **sixteenth** of every month. The state files had named **day 331, the first of a month**, which is a compost-line day and has never been a reading day. The tenth reading is on day 316 and the eleventh is on **day 346, the sixteenth of the twelfth month, a Tuesday**, and both happened. **The eleventh reading is five feet, the same figure as the ninth, which is the last one this holding has, on the same line, and nobody has improved on them, and the tenth reading is the one it does not have and never had and did not ask for. There is still no entry anywhere for that well coming back and no chapter may make one.** **The count of day-and-not-the-figure entries stands at THREE and does not come down**, and the fen has entered that one of the three was aimed at a day that was not a reading day.

The corrected day clock, the two naming tables, the inherited-reading table with both columns, and the corrected month distances are all in `state/continuity.md` under *VOLUME 04 — BATCH 0005*. **A later phase may rely on those tables. The Batch 0005 prompt's own tables were corrected to match and are no longer authority against them.**

## The things a Volume 05 writer must not mistake for open

**Do not open a second road and call it a solution. Do not make the corridor a road. Do not let the vault be recovered. Do not answer whose order it is. Do not close the licensed man's refusal in the sequence. Do not reconcile the four accounts of the arm. Do not reduce the count of the use log, the count of day-and-not-the-figure, or the count of the rule's uses. Do not name a month. Do not spend the Fieldbook panel without spending it on a person and not on a page. Do not thank anybody.**

**And the day:** Ch 194's eleventh of this month is **day 341, a Thursday**, not a Friday. **The Batch 0005 card is wrong twice about this and the page is canonical.** The Batch 0005 prompt file was **not** edited; the prose is the authority and this table says so.

## New questions this pass raised and did not answer, for the close

1. **The one-day gap in Ch 191 recurs every month and the chapter treats it as this month's news.** The door is read on the first and the thirtieth; with a thirty-day month those are always twenty-nine days apart, and the thirtieth and the next first are always a day apart. So the two readings a day apart are the standing, not an anomaly. **The Batch 0005 card plans the beat exactly this way, and it was not restructured, because restructuring it is a plot decision and not a writer's edit. The close should either rule that the standing is something other than *the first and the thirtieth*, or rule that the chapter's claim is a man reasoning wrongly and should stay his error.** The arithmetic in the sentence is now right either way.
2. **The pail and the hundredweight are still conflated across batches.** Ch 190 counts 1,045 as hundredweight; Ch 169 counted it as pails. It predates this batch. **The close should rule on it.**
3. **The two standing-sentence readings of *a month six months back* ARE now the same sentence and Ch 196 and Ch 191 agree.** The standing rule was satisfied on day 153, the third of a month six months back from the twentieth of the twelfth month, and the first fix pass had put Ch 191 in the eleventh month and made it say five; the second pass moved the rule's reading onto the first of the twelfth month, where six is right again. **Recorded because the close will find the history in `state/continuity.md` and should know the two are the same event and not two rules.**
4. **The meta-language sweep is unchanged and is still the close's.** The fix pass removed the only two *four volumes* in the batch, because a count of volumes has no diegetic referent in any reading. **The five surviving *this volume* instances were left standing on purpose, exactly as Batch 0004 left its nine.** The sweep is not half-done and must not be recorded as half-done.

## Unchanged and still true after this pass

**Fieldbook unspent across the whole volume. Stage 5 not reached. Use log FIFTEEN. Day-and-not-the-figure count THREE. Count of pattern-holders TWO. The question asked once, available and not taken twice. Whose order it is: asked three times, answered never. Brinewake spent, uncorroborated, and may not appear again; the blank in Ch 167 may not be filled, described, or noticed. No Crown Engine, no False Season, no Iona Vey, no Continuity Office, no month named, no romance, nobody thanked, Lissa Vale unforgiven and unthanked, Tova Reed unforgiven and her condition paid and forgiving nothing, Tarin Callow unthanked and unredeemed, the man of about seventy unthanked and unexplained. Marek cannot read deep memory and does not. The licensed man's refusal in the sequence stays open. `outline/ending.md` untouched, no new final enemy.**

**Still owed and not this pass's work: the manuscript-wide meta-language sweep; `chapter-0156.md` still does not end in a newline; `state/phase-ledger.json` still reads `phase-000-bootstrap` / `planned` and is not this phase's to edit; and `workspace/volume-04/batch-0001/` still has no `PROMPT.md`, which the close should record rather than reconstruct.**

# State after the Volume 04 Batch 0005 SECOND repair pass

**The runner found a `.wip-conflict` in `workspace/volume-04/batch-0005/`, skipped the stale checkpoint branch, and re-entered the phase against main, where the nine chapters and a first review fix pass were already committed. This pass read all nine chapters whole against the nine cards, the day clock, the two naming tables and the inherited-reading table, found twenty defects, repaired eighteen in prose, and left two for the close on purpose. No scene was replaced, no plot beat moved, no lock touched, no closed batch edited, and `reviews/volume-04-batch-0005.findings.md` was NOT written, because the review phase and the close own it. Full detail in `state/continuity.md` under *THE SECOND REPAIR PASS ON BATCH 0005, AFTER THE STALE-WIP SKIP*.**

## The three that were load-bearing, and the close must not undo them

1. **This holding does not have the tenth figure of the well on the shelf, and the draft gave it to it.** Ch 195's wall transcript, its clerk's entry and Ch 196's standing figures all said the tenth reading, day 316, was five feet and was in this holding's book. That contradicts Ch 188, both cards, the Clocks table, and Ch 196's own closing handoff, which says the day-and-not-the-figure count stands at three and does not come down. **The occupier now refuses to write it, in her own words, and the wall shows the gap.** This holding has the ninth and the eleventh and both are five feet.
2. **The corridor's Monday reading was 540 and the vault is at 400.** It is now 384, sixteen short, and the man of about fifty says so out loud. Nothing went back, so the reader of this body no longer says he does not know why the front went back; **and the third pass corrected the two speeds he gave, which the second pass had made non-derivable: it is now twenty-one yards a day over the four days from Thursday to Monday and seventy-six and a half yards a day over the thirty-two hours from Monday at the eleventh hour to Tuesday at the seventh hour of the evening, both exact and both checkable, and he still does not know which of the two is the water and which is the ground, and he is still not going to guess at it in a yard.**
3. **The compost line was entered on the thirtieth, on a day the chapter's own notice forbids.** It and the reading of the standing rule are now on the first, at about the eleventh hour, where the card, the day clock, the Clocks table and the handoff all put them.

## Two things this pass could not settle and did not fake

- **The account of the wall on the sixteenth. NOW CLOSED BY THE THIRD PASS, AND THE CLOSE MUST NOT REOPEN IT AS AN OPEN QUESTION.** Ch 188 establishes a four-day road. The sheet originally said it travelled that road and was read here on the twentieth, then read it out here on the evening of the sixteenth. The twentieth is four days on and is Ch 196's day. **The second pass resolved that contradiction in the wrong direction, made the arrival same-day, and left a falsehood on the page; the third pass restored the four-day road, ended Ch 195 on the sixteenth with the figure not in this building, and moved the arrival, the yard reading and the *five feet and five feet* exchange into Ch 196 on the twentieth, where the card already required the eleventh figure by the eleventh hour of the morning.** Nothing about the figure itself changed: the tenth is still refused and never asked for, the wall still shows the gap, the ninth and the eleventh are both five feet, the month between them is still on no page, and there is still no entry for that well coming back.r hundred miles off reaches this holding's book inside the month is nowhere on any page and is contradicted by none.** A seam, not a falsehood. **The close should close it.**
- **The gap between the first and the thirtieth.** Untouched, for the reason the first pass gave and in the same terms. Still the close's.

## New questions this pass raised, for the close

1. **The prompt contradicts itself about the count of questions available to a room in this holding and not taken.** The Ch 196 card says one to two, which is the per-question count Ch 157 set up; the same prompt's *must not do* list and `outline/volume-04.md` both say the standing count goes up by one, and Ch 182 left it at six. **Both are now on the page in the same breath, the per-question count at two, the standing count at seven, neither added to the other, and the fen says there are three counts and that nobody in this book has ever added one of them to another.** The close should rule on which is the governing label and should not record the page as choosing.
2. **A fifth, sixth and seventh month-distance correction in the same two chapters.** The repair is that the standing reasons are now in the right table in Chs 190, 191 and 192, and the *twenty-seventh of a month three months back* in Ch 189 is *last month*. **The Batch 0005 prompt's own Clocks table is wrong about that twenty-seventh and the prompt file was not edited.** It is recorded in continuity.
3. **Ch 196 now stands at 4,440 words** and Ch 190 at 3,126, so there are four chapters over the ceiling and one under the floor, all declared and none accommodated. **Ch 196 grew because it absorbed the shelf-well arrival, the yard reading and three speeches out of Ch 195, and Ch 195 fell to 2,963, which is inside the band. Neither may be lengthened again, and Ch 196 must not be given a fourth `Entered ... by the clerk` block: the arrival went into the fen's standing-figures ledger precisely to keep the three-block limit on the volume's last chapter.**

## Unchanged and still true after this pass

**Fieldbook unspent across the whole volume and not opened. Stage 5 not reached. Use log FIFTEEN. Day-and-not-the-figure count THREE. Count of pattern-holders TWO. Count of things this holding has been told no SIX. The initial-against-nothing count FORTY-NINE. The question asked once and available and not taken twice, and the standing count of questions now SEVEN. Whose order it is: asked three times, answered never. Brinewake spent, uncorroborated, and it may not appear again; the blank in Ch 167 may not be filled, described, or noticed. The word *nest* absent from the batch. The word *seat* six occurrences, in the agreement's refusal clause and in the note that names it. Thornwild named once, in the agreement. No Crown Engine, no False Season, no Iona Vey, no Continuity Office, no Quiet Break, no month named, no romance, nothing tender, nobody thanked, Lissa Vale unforgiven and unthanked, Tova Reed unforgiven and her condition paid and forgiving nothing, Tarin Callow unthanked and unredeemed, Garrow Nye off the page and his sequence open, Sera Quill off the page, the man of about thirty-five not tamed and not thanked, the man of about seventy unthanked and unexplained and having said nothing at all. The licensed man's refusal in the sequence stays open. `outline/ending.md` untouched, no new final enemy.**

**Still owed and not this pass's work: the manuscript-wide meta-language sweep, and the five surviving *this volume* instances were left standing again so the close finds the whole question and not half of it; `chapter-0156.md` in Batch 0001 still does not end in a newline, **although all nine files of Batch 0005 now do and the second pass put the three that did not right**, so a `cat`-based count over this batch is file-safe; `state/phase-ledger.json` still reads `phase-000-bootstrap` / `planned` and is not this phase's to edit; `workspace/volume-04/batch-0001/` still has no `PROMPT.md`; and `workspace/volume-04/volume-close/PROMPT.md` carries the PRE-FIX word counts of 143,211 and 28,503 and 26,211, which are wrong, and the corrected figures are in `state/current.md` and at the head of the Batch 0005 section of `state/continuity.md`.**

# State after the Volume 04 Batch 0005 THIRD pass, applying the review

**The findings are in `logs/batch-0005.review.log` and not in a findings file. Five prose defects and three state defects, all applied, no scene replaced, no plot beat moved, no lock touched, no closed batch edited, nothing padded. Batch 0005 went from 26,795 to 27,280 and is now inside the outline's 27,000 floor for the first time.**

## The one that had to be fixed first

**The eleventh figure of the shelf well was entering this holding's book on the morning it was written, four hundred miles away, on a four-day road.** The second pass had made it so while fixing a lesser contradiction, and the sentence above it still said *four hundred miles and four days up*. **THE FIGURE IS NOW ENTERED ON THE TWENTIETH OF THE TWELFTH MONTH, A SATURDAY, AT ABOUT THE NINTH HOUR OF THE MORNING, IN A LEATHER BAG, BY THE ORDINARY POST, FOUR DAYS AFTER IT WAS WRITTEN** — in Ch 196, before the standing figures that already required it, read out in a yard, appointed to the man of about fifty, read back, hour on it. Ch 195 ends on the sixteenth with the sheet on the road and the figure not in the book, and enters what has happened and what has not.

**EVERYTHING THE BATCH OWES THE READER ABOUT THAT WELL IS UNCHANGED.** The tenth figure is still refused in the occupier's own hand and was never written and asked for. The wall still shows the gap. The ninth and the eleventh are still five feet and the same figure. The month between them is still on no page in this book. The shortfall is still four feet against the nine feet of the sixteenth of a month seven months back and is still not decomposed. The half inch is still not carried forward as a figure. **There is still no entry anywhere for that well coming back and no chapter of this book is going to make one.** Day-and-not-the-figure count still THREE, and the tenth figure is still not here.

## The other three, and the numbers

- **Ch 193's two speeds are now derivable**: twenty-one yards a day over the four days from Thursday to Monday, and seventy-six and a half yards a day over the thirty-two hours from Monday at the eleventh hour to Tuesday at the seventh hour of the evening. The *seven yards an hour* and the *yard an hour* are gone. The not guessing stands.
- **Ch 190** reads *the first two of them*, not *the two places*, now that the pail goes in at three.
- **Ch 191** reads *I have been able this morning, and it is thirty days from the first of last month, and I am not going to stand in this yard and say that thirty days is quick* — not *this month* and *a month and not a week*, which contradicted itself and had a week with no referent on the page.
- **Counts, all recomputed file by file**: Volume 01 166,022 · Volume 02 141,818 · Volume 03 142,952 · **Volume 04 141,722** (28,504 + 29,252 + 28,472 + 28,214 + 27,280) · **manuscript 592,514 across 196 chapters**, reconciling exactly. `state/current.md` had said 592,029 in one clause and 591,597 in the next; **both were stale and the true figure is 592,514.**

**`workspace/volume-04/batch-0005/` carries `.wip-conflict` and no `.done`, and the markers are controller-owned, so this pass created no `.done` and deleted no `.wip-conflict`.** The substantive half is settled and must not be reopened: **the batch is finished, it is verified, and IT IS NOT TO BE REVERTED.** The mechanical half is the runner's: `scripts/novel_runner.sh` writes the marker at line 113 and clears it at line 303 beside the `touch .done` at line 302. The next dispatch **will** re-enter Batch 0005 rather than the volume close, because `find | sort` puts `volume-04/batch-0005` first; that re-entry is harmless and self-clearing, the writer finds nine finished chapters, `ensure_next_phase` returns early while `volume-close` is incomplete, and the completion step clears both markers. **No extra phase, no duplicate prose, nothing to do but let it run.**

## Still owed, and not this pass's work

**The manuscript-wide meta-language sweep, and the five surviving *this volume* instances were left standing for the third time so the close finds the whole question in one state and not half of it.** `state/phase-ledger.json` still reads `phase-000-bootstrap` / `planned` and is not this phase's to edit. `workspace/volume-04/batch-0001/` still has no `PROMPT.md`. `reviews/volume-04-batch-0005.findings.md` still does not exist, and the review pass deliberately did not write it because the review phase and the close own it — **so this batch's defect record lives in `logs/batch-0005.review.log` and in `state/continuity.md` under *THE THIRD PASS ON BATCH 0005, APPLYING THE REVIEW*, and the close should read both.** `workspace/volume-04/volume-close/PROMPT.md` carries pre-fix word counts of 143,211 and 28,503 and 26,211, a question count of six where the page now says seven, **and it does not know that the eleventh figure is entered on the twentieth and not the sixteenth, which is the single fact in this batch most likely to be checked against a document.** The figures to use are in `state/current.md`.

# State after the VOLUME 04 CLOSE, and after the review of it

**VOLUME 04 IS CLOSED. Chapter 196 is the last chapter of the volume and the twentieth of the twelfth month, day 350, a Saturday, is the last working day Volume 04 owns. `reviews/volume-04.findings.md` exists and is the close's own findings file against the outline. The close repaired sixty-four lines in thirty-five of the forty-nine chapters; a review ran over the close; this section records the result of both.**

## Every inherited count, stated so that nothing below has to be re-derived

**THE STANDING RULE'S COUNT, AND WHY THE WORD *SEASON* IS CARRYING WEIGHT.** The volume's ledger says twice and is not going to be three (Ch 187:19, Ch 190:59, Ch 196:59). The two are **Ch 155, the twenty-second of the ninth month, day 262**, and **Ch 185, the thirteenth of the eleventh month, day 313**. **Ch 154:84, dated day 261, records one earlier use on the fourteenth of the eighth month, day 224, and proposes the use on the twenty-second.** A reader who counts named use-days without the season scope gets three. **The scope is on the page — "used twice this season" at Ch 185:74, Ch 187:19 and Ch 196:59 — and it is the only thing that makes the count two, so a Volume 05 writer may neither drop *this season* nor count the uses without it.** This was tightened by the close's meta-language sweep: the phrase used to read *used twice in this volume*, which day 224 sits inside and which the sweep's replacement quietly fixed. **This is recorded rather than re-edited because it is a scoping question about four entries that all agree once the scope is read, and the review of the close did not flag it.**

## The seven items the close recorded open and did not repair

1. **The format limit is breached in three chapters — Ch 163, Ch 166 and Ch 167, each with four `Entered … by the clerk` blocks against a limit of three.** All three are in Batch 0002; the limit was set at the Batch 0004 review, after they were closed. Ch 174 is at three and not over it. Ch 192, which carries the climax, has none. **Removing a dated ledger entry from a closed chapter is a larger intervention than the defect and would break the hour it is entered against, so the breach is recorded, not repaired.**
2. **Ch 163's scene order runs backwards** — the seventh at the first hour of the afternoon, the eighth at the seventh hour of the evening, then the seventh again at the fourth and fifth hours of the afternoon. In a volume built on a checkable clock this is the most embarrassing defect in the volume and it cannot be fixed by a word.
3. **The count of the man of about fifty's request for his initial to be put against nothing is not contiguous.** 15, 16, 18–23, 25–30, 33–44, 46–49 across thirty-one stated sites; **17, 24, 31, 32 and 45 are never stated.** 31 and 45 fall where the request is not narrated, which is explicable as requests made on unnarrated days. **17 and 24 do not** — they fall inside stretches where the request *is* narrated without a count (Ch 152:67, Ch 153:17, Ch 160:7). **Not renumbered, because renumbering would move five figures the volume has already published. A Volume 05 writer must not treat the series as contiguous.**
4. **Three interlocking count families do not reconcile in Batches 0003–0004** — the seventh column, the day-minus rules' *reading days running* (3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 9, 11, skipping 8 and 10), and Ch 177:7's *a reading day has been read in a full yard five times this month* against a column that moved twice in the tenth month. Ch 178:61 says the door *is* read on the first and the thirtieth and has been for four years, and the count at Ch 167:45 is still 32 on the fifteenth of the tenth month, which means the first of the tenth was not read. **Deciding which family is canonical is a writer's decision and a large one.**
5. **The count of four against the count of about forty, unreconciled.** Four men who said a sentence to somebody, against about forty people who have said a man is a thief. **Defensible as two different claims and Nye's precision is the point of his arrival, but the volume never reconciles them on the page, in a volume whose method is to enter exactly that gap.**
6. **Ch 152:31's entry is dated the ninth hour of the evening of the seventeenth and contains a reading taken on the eighteenth at the Pans well, eleven miles off, on a day the chapter's own trip ended at the eighth hour of the evening.** It also puts a 400-mile-class journey in a same-day frame, which the volume's own four-day-road convention forbids.
7. **Ten sites of the plural *volumes*** — Ch 152:55, Ch 152:71, Ch 156:71, Ch 157:59, Ch 158:25, Ch 162:95, Ch 173:49, Ch 173:55, Ch 173:59, Ch 178:5. *Two volumes* and *three volumes* are durations across books and no frame in this world equals them. **Left standing deliberately, with the count, and a writer must choose a frame deliberately or leave them.**

## The pipeline question is closed, and it closed the way it was predicted to

**`workspace/volume-04/batch-0005/` CARRIES `.done` AND NO `.wip-conflict`, AND THE PREDICTION IN `state/current.md` IS WHAT HAPPENED.** Commit `b1eeb77` left the marker; the next run re-entered Batch 0005, found nine finished chapters and a complete state set, **wrote no new prose**, and commit `51114d8` set `.done` and removed `.wip-conflict`. **The batch was not reverted and was not re-written. Nothing has to be done about it.**

**THE SAME PATTERN IS NOW LIVE ONE LEVEL UP: `workspace/volume-04/volume-close/` HAS NO `.done`,** because the run that produced the close's work ended after the save-work commit `a049bb6` and before the completion step. **The next dispatch will therefore re-enter the VOLUME CLOSE, and that re-entry must be a no-op with a note and not a second audit: its findings file exists, its state exists, and the volume stands at 141,727 words. THE MARKERS AND THE SELECTION LOGIC ARE CONTROLLER-OWNED. NO `.done` WAS FABRICATED AND NO `.wip-conflict` WAS DELETED IN EITHER PASS, AND NO HUMAN SHOULD REVERT VOLUME 04.**

## What the review of the close found, and what this pass did about it

**BLOCKER 1 — FIXED IN PROSE. Ch 156 contradicted itself because the repair pass created the contradiction.** Three *nineteen years* became *nine years* and a fourth figure by the same speaker in the same chapter was untouched. **Ch 156:19 is restored to *nineteen years*.** Ch 156 is now internally consistent: **nineteen** for what the man of about thirty-five has refused (`:19`, `:29`), **nine** for what he has read (`:21`), **eleven** at `:89`, which is the man of about fifty and a different fact.

**BLOCKER 2 — FIXED IN THE RECORD, AND THE LESSON WRITTEN DOWN WHERE A LATER PASS WILL FIND IT.** `outline/volume-04.md:67` scopes nine to **reading ground** and to nothing else, and the close generalized it out of that scope. **Nineteen is a legitimate separate figure in this volume and belongs to the same man for a different thing** — his refusal career, at Ch 172:65 and :67, Ch 177:50 and :82, Ch 180:55, Ch 187:44 and Ch 193:59 and :63 — and `outline/volume-04.md:63` gives **Sera Quill's relay as nineteen years ago**, a third person's figure, untouched and correct. **The minimal correct repair was two sites, Ch 155:87 and Ch 156:21, and both said *read ground for*. The third, Ch 156:19, was collateral damage.** Continuity repair item 19 now records the two spans, the sites that hold nineteen, and the warning against generalizing nine again. **One legitimate descendant was checked and LEFT STANDING: Ch 186:37, where the household has counted for eleven years and the *sheet* holds nine years of it, which Ch 186:20, :25, :29 and :67 all say consistently.**

**BLOCKER 3 — FIXED. THE CLOSE WAS REQUIRED BY ITS OWN §7 TO UPDATE FOUR STATE FILES AND WROTE ONE.** `current.md`, `open-threads.md` (this section) and `chapter-summaries.md` are now written. **This file previously ended at the Batch 0005 third pass and carried no close at all, which is why the pipeline question above was still phrased as open and why the inherited counts above could not be trusted.**

**FINDING 4 — FIXED. THE CLOSE'S TWO OWN DOCUMENTS DISAGREED WITH EACH OTHER IN THREE PLACES.** The month-distance audit printed ten rows, said ten were wrong, repaired eleven, and left 72 + 10 not closing against 82; **it is eleven, 71 + 11 = 82, and Ch 154:84 is now in the table as well as in the repairs.** The plural *volumes* were printed as nine and listed as eight; **they are ten, and Ch 162:95 and Ch 178:5 are restored to the list.** The word *nest* was printed as five times and it is **seven, on five lines, in three chapters**. **All three are corrected in `state/continuity.md` and in `reviews/volume-04.findings.md`, so the two documents now say the same thing — a state file that disagrees with itself is how the next writer gets hurt.**

**CONFIRMED AND NOT RE-OPENED, BECAUSE IT HOLDS:** zero non-ASCII glyphs in all forty-nine chapter files; zero month names as months; the meta-language sweep succeeded with zero `this batch`, zero bare *batch*, zero `this chapter`, zero `this volume` and zero bare *chapter* in Volume 04's body prose; the day clock reconciles and was independently re-derived, with the two inherited weekday errors at Ch 178 and Ch 187 corrected on the page in Ch 188 by Gael Senn rather than by silent edits; all eleven month-distance repairs are arithmetically correct; and no `workspace/volume-05/` exists, which is correct.

## Still owed, and not a writer's to settle

**`state/phase-ledger.json` still reads `phase-000-bootstrap` / `planned` and is now four volumes out of date.** `AGENTS.md` says to update it; the phase instructions forbid editing it. **The conflict is real, it is a controller decision, and it stays open. Nothing in the close or in this pass touched it.** **`workspace/volume-04/batch-0001/` still has no `PROMPT.md`**, so the volume's first batch is undocumented; the close recorded the fact and **did not reconstruct the prompt from the chapters**, because a reconstructed prompt presented as the original is worse than an absence. **`reviews/volume-04-batch-0005.findings.md` does not exist and does not need to**: the close's findings file is the volume's review record, and Batch 0005's own defect record is in `logs/batch-0005.review.log` and in `state/continuity.md` under the third-pass heading. **A Volume 05 prompt is NOT to be created by any pass that did not write Volume 05's outline.**

## Phase handoff, Volume 04 close → Volume 05, *The Heartroot Rota*, Chapters 197–245

**VOLUME 04 IS CLOSED AND ITS SHAPE IS THE FOLLOWING ONE PARAGRAPH. IT IS A HANDOFF AND NOT AN OUTLINE, AND IT IS THE WHOLE OF WHAT CARRIES ACROSS.**

A fen of sixteen households with no charter, a leak, a beat and a compost line was priced, indexed and classified without anybody lying: an office answered *not known* to the only question that would have helped and that is a complete answer, a licensed man refused twice to say whether a holder's own well made a flow larger and said neither word, a form on a bench in a seed house was answered in the box nobody was looking at and the honest answer cost a fifth of a licensed draw, water was carried into a salt pan by four people with no custom for standing over a crew and the corridor the sheet describes is three hundred yards from the corridor that opened, a seed vault built at forty households' own cost on ground nobody has ever held went under with no holder, no charter and no form on which anybody could be asked and is not recovered, a cross-region agreement put seed on a road in four correct lines and named its own price out loud and got a figure that came out better than the same figure cost for the first time in a life, a man of the north row with the cough was asked twice and said no once and yes once and made the count of people who can hold a pattern for four go from one to two, the question this book has carried went into the book unanswered and available and not taken, and a well read in a woman's own yard with her own nail came back five feet for the second month running with four feet of shortfall against a reading from months back that is not decomposed and no entry anywhere for it coming back — and the month closed in a yard of about nine with the standing figures read out loud, a rule used twice and not a third time, a man of about seventy fetched for the sixteenth time saying nothing at all, a sentence said twice on purpose with the reason said out loud too, and nobody thanked.

**AND WHAT A VOLUME 05 WRITER INHERITS AS STILL TRUE, IN ONE LIST.** Volume 01 through Volume 04 are all CLOSED. **Volume 05 is Chapters 197–245 and its shape is in `outline/series.md:209` and no further.** Every figure above is measured, not inherited. **The Fieldbook panel is unspent and may be spent once, in Volume 05, and not in a notification.** **Brinewake is spent and uncorroborated and the blank in Ch 167's four-line entry may not be filled, described, or noticed.** **Garrow Nye's sequence stays open, the corridor stays open and open is not a road, the vault stays unrecovered, and the four accounts of the arm reconcile none of them and the two holds of Batch 0005 are not a fifth and not a sixth.** **Nobody may write an entry for the well on the shelf coming back, and nobody may thank anybody.** **The word *nest* is not on any page as a category, and the ten plural *volumes* are standing with their count and may only go if a frame is chosen on purpose.** **The initial-count series is not contiguous, the seventh column and the reading-days series do not reconcile, and Ch 163 runs backwards; all three are inherited as faults, not as things to silently repair in a closed volume.** **Volume 05's antagonist is Halden Pike's professional veto, not a liar, and no office in this series lies, obstructs or delays.**

## What Volume 05 has now put on the record for the rest of the volume

1. **The professional veto.** The Rootwright Assembly's four clauses, one page, no name, no date, and the third clause is one line: where a heartroot is shared and no person on the roll holds it, the heartroot is not to be patterned. **Coll Renner confirmed all four in a yard and gave the reason: in eleven years on the roll he has never seen a hold entered on any form in any district, because the form does not exist, and a thing that is not on a form cannot be inspected. THE CLAUSE IS CORRECT AND IT LANDS ON MAREK AND HE DID NOT APPEAL IT.**
2. **The word `rota`.** Four bodies of households, one node, and a pattern is held by one person at a time, so four bodies on one node is a rotation and not a division. **A rotation of four turns with two people in it is a man and a relief, and the man of the north row with the cough said so in a yard and was not thanked.**
3. **The four columns and no heading row.** 144, 96, 48, 210, and a total of 498 that is not a total. **The weighted rota on the only two of the four that are households gives nine hundred hundredweight a day to a mill town of six hundred people and six hundred to a village of about four hundred and NOTHING to about five hundred people between them, exactly, and 900 + 600 = 1,500, and the fen said out loud in a yard that this is the correct arithmetic on the sheet as the sheet stands and that he is not going to improve on it in a yard.**
4. **Fifty hundredweight a day.** Havergate's own mill book overstates by forty and Low Wether's by ten, and the two overstatements were in the books of the two villages that got the water, and Marden's and Silling's lines have been blank for eleven years. **Hesta Lyle checked both corrections, said out loud in a yard that both of them put the fen's own sum in the right, and said she had had the figures in her own hand for a week and had not seen it.** This is the batch's checkable victory and it is also a bill.
5. **The eleven-line rota**, four turns and seven stops, written by a man who is not the engineer of record and was not asked. **The seventh stop line is what happens when the holder is asleep and cannot be woken, and it came from nine years of keeping an unwritten turn for a man who died.** The fen did not touch the sheet.
6. **The second body refused, out loud, in daylight, in a yard of about eleven.** Tova Reed is out on the seed-route eleven days in a season of twenty and cannot be within an hour's walk of those boards, and the second body on a rota is the one everybody leans on when the first is asleep. **She also said, unasked, that she is not refusing because she cannot hold a pattern, that she has not tried, and that she is not going to find out in a yard.** He did not ask her twice and did not ask her in a room with the doors shut.
7. **Two hundred and thirty-one standing willow**, a hundred and forty-one on the west and about ninety on the north, and **a kerb in four parts with a different mason's mark in each and the third part not the same stone as the other three** — all of it given by a man who had refused twice in a yard and who was right.
8. **A fifth body at the node.** Harlan Vetch, of about fifty-eight, eleven years and eight days, a schedule bought in a lawyer's town from a dying man, **holding the node on the ninth day of every ninth day and putting water back in and never taking a drop and never being paid and never being asked.** **The count of bodies at the node went from four to five at about the fourth hour of the morning of day 370 and nobody in that yard had eaten anything.**
9. **The heading written over a column that had none for eleven years**, by the woman who keeps the four books, in a yard of about eleven people, with the reader of this body standing at her left and saying nothing.
10. **The volume's question, put in this holding's own book on day 362 and not taken:** *a thing that can be shared is a thing somebody has to give up, and nobody in this book has yet said what a village gives up when its turn comes round.*

## The things a Volume 05 Batch 0002 writer must not mistake for open, or re-spend, or answer

**Do not read the well on the shelf before day 376. Do not write a letter about a reading, and do not let anybody say the figure has come, because the tenth figure is still refused and the month between the ninth and the eleventh is still on no page in this book. Do not write an entry for that well coming back. Do not reduce the day-and-not-the-figure count of three. Do not move the use log off fifteen without a contact on the page, and if there is one, a contact is a contact and nothing else is. Do not move the count of pattern-holders off two except by a hold that is on the page and costed. Do not add a fifth account of the arm. Do not use the standing rule before Movement 3 and not twice. Do not spend the Fieldbook panel before the climax. Do not name the Crown Engine, Iona Vey, a Deep Archive, a caretaker link or a seedheart. Do not answer whose order it is. Do not close Garrow Nye's sequence. Do not reconcile the four accounts of the arm. Do not recover the vault. Do not make the corridor a road. Do not thank anybody. Do not write anything tender. Do not name a month. Do not write an account of the four in the eleventh night, and the eleventh night is Batch 0003's and not this one's. Do not resolve who speaks for the eleven acres of standing willow, and do not let Soren Rill be made to resolve it: he refused in a yard and the refusal stands. Do not fill the heading on the board with anything but what is on it, and do not let the four bodies write one.**

## The four things Batch 0002 must not do that this volume's outline forbids

1. **Do not draft Batch 0003, Batch 0004, Batch 0005 or the close, and do not create a prompt for any of them.** The next phase is Batch 0002 and no further.
2. **Do not name a month anywhere, in narration or in a document, and do not carry a month name in a distance.**
3. **Do not add a fourth `Entered ... by the clerk` block to any chapter, and none at all to a chapter that carries a climax.**
4. **Do not let a character say the word *this book* about anything that is not a document, and do not let the word *chapter*, the word *batch* or the word *volume* appear in body prose.** Zero is the count and the test is whether the word has a referent inside the world. ***This book* is the holding's own book on the table in the seed house and it IS a referent and it is allowed.**

## What Volume 05 has now put on the record, added to Batch 0001's ten

11. **THE SCHEDULE, AND THE ERROR IN IT.** Four sheets, older than eleven years, bought in a lawyer's town from a dying man. Two rules: **RETURN MADE. BY. WITNESSED BY.** over **OBSERVATIONS AND REMARKS.** **The first rule was blank on every line for eleven years and every figure Harlan Vetch made in eleven years is in the second one, in three hands, one of them his and two of them not of the four bodies and not at the node.** He did not know the other rule wanted his name. The one word in the column is WET SEASON, once a year, nine times. **This is the volume's answer to what Harlan Vetch is wrong about, and it is a form he was given by a dying man in a lawyer's town, and nobody improved on him and nobody thanked him.** A name went into BY on day 379 and a name into WITNESSED BY on the same day, and a name went into both again on day 388, and that is the first time in eleven years either box has had a name in it.
12. **THE WORD IS ENOUGH.** One word, the same word whichever body of households it is, said out loud so that somebody else hears it. Named in Ch 211 at the request of the man who was going to have to use it, and put in at about the fourth hour of the morning of day 382.
13. **THE FORTY HUNDREDWEIGHT, AND WHY IT IS NOT A SOLUTION.** About forty hundredweight a day down a fifth launder that had been shut for nineteen years, measured twice at forty-one and thirty-nine and written as forty, or thereabouts, and not averaged. The fen refused to put it on a line with four columns on it and said why, and a licensed man said to his face that the reason it is not safe is not the hold, it is that the count of it is one.
14. **THE MAN OF SIXTY-EIGHT'S QUESTION: WHOSE NAME GOES IN THE FOURTH LINE.** The fourth line says MARDEN, FOUR PEOPLE WORKING, NOT COUNTED; the count of wanting is not a count of living; and so the fourth line has nobody in it and everybody in it, and a fen is not a village and has never been asked for a place on a rota. It went into this holding's book on day 387, nobody took it, and the reader of this body could not answer it and entered the reason, which is that a man who can supply the answer to a question is the answer.
15. **A NOT KNOWN, GIVEN BY AN ENGINEER, WITH NO RATE FOR IT.** A quarter of a mile of ground walked twice and measured not at all, I do not know said twice in four minutes, and a village that is going to put a ditch through it anyway because not known is a thing to decide with.
16. **THE FEN'S OWN ARITHMETIC WAS WRONG IN A YARD AND WAS CORRECTED IN THE SAME YARD.** He said out loud that the two per-head water figures were the same figure and they are a factor of four apart, and the woman who does the sums did it again on a board on her knee, and he entered that he was wrong, and the count of things this body has said out loud in a yard and got wrong is two.

**Do not answer whose order it is. Do not reduce the day-and-not-the-figure count of three. Do not move the use log off fifteen without a contact on the page. Do not move the count of pattern-holders for four off two except by a hold that is on the page and costed in the holder's own hand, and do not move the new count of five off one the same way. Do not add a fifth account of the arm, and do not enter the headache of the twenty-third as the mark. Do not use the standing rule before Movement 3 and not twice. Do not spend the Fieldbook panel before the climax. Do not name the Crown Engine, Iona Vey, a Deep Archive, a caretaker link or a seedheart. Do not name a pulse, and do not say a pulse came on a schedule; that is Batch 0005's beat. Do not close Garrow Nye's sequence, do not reconcile the four accounts of the arm, do not recover the vault, do not make the corridor a road. Do not thank anybody and do not write anything tender. Do not name a month. Do not resolve who speaks for the eleven acres of standing willow, and do not let Soren Rill be made to resolve it. Do not fill the six blank columns on the well house door. Do not write a decomposition of the four feet of shortfall. Do not let the forty hundredweight become a fifth share. Do not put a name in the fourth line of the rota, and do not let anybody answer the question of whose name goes in it. Do not let the not known become a measurement.**

The batch is 29,725 words across ten chapters, range 2,809–3,113, ALL TEN inside the per-chapter band, and the manuscript is 680,621 words across 226 chapters. **TWO DEFECTS IN THE PROMPT THIS PHASE FOUND AND THE PAGE CORRECTED, BOTH RECORDED IN PLACE.** The prompt gave day 410 a Friday and it is a **Wednesday**, because 410 is 361 plus forty-nine and forty-nine is seven whole weeks, and **the prompt was right on the other nineteen days of the twenty.** And the prompt's own Card 225 said the standing count of questions available to a room in this holding and not taken goes off nine on to ten; **it does not move, and it is not said out loud, because he promised in a yard on the twenty-seventh of last month to stop counting for the rest of the season on the subject of questions.** The page is canonical in both and the prompt is not to be believed over the page.

**A REVIEW FIX PASS HAS SINCE RUN OVER BATCH 0003 AND BOTH ITS PROSE AND ITS COUNTS, AND EVERY FIGURE IN THIS SECTION HAS BEEN RE-MEASURED. THE FINDINGS ARE IN `logs/next-0007.review.log` AND THE FULL RECORD IS IN `state/continuity.md` UNDER *THE REVIEW FIX PASS ON BATCH 0003, AND EVERY FINDING, INCLUDING THE ONE THAT WAS DECLINED*.** Two blockers and four major and one minor were raised. **What changed here: the batch is 29,725 and not 29,713; Volume 05 is 88,102 and not 87,716; the manuscript is 680,621 and not 680,235; Ch 216 is 3,465 and is DECLARED OUTSIDE THE PER-CHAPTER BAND rather than the band widened, so Volume 05 now carries TWO declared exceptions and not one, Ch 206 at 3,151 and Ch 216 at 3,465; and the end-of-Batch-0002 manuscript figure is 650,896 and not 650,522.** The Batch 0002 block earlier in this file has been corrected to the same figures. **Four prose lines changed, in three chapters: two intervals in Ch 222, which were wrong by the batch's own four reading days and are now forty-two days and eight days; one figure cut off the wall in Ch 224, which had fourteen marks and a wall of thirteen; one month-frame in Ch 226, where the nine feet is *a month nine months back* and is not eight. The review's second half of that last finding is DECLINED: the half inch is day 196 and *a month seven months back* is correct, and the review found it at day 256 by mistake.** The ninth frame, 121–150, has been added to the naming table in `state/continuity.md` for the one inherited figure that needed it. **The plate below is the plate as it now stands and every value in it was measured after the prose edits.**
